  - Longest window: 2,670 s (44:30), every 90-min slot; S7-D9's `TimeoutStartSec` is therefore 2,670 s + 120 s = 2,790 s (46:30): `Schedule::longest_window` derives it and `services/newsvault/tests/deploy.rs::every_run_bound_is_the_longest_window_plus_two_minutes` holds the collect unit, the refresh unit and both Kubernetes jobs to it.

- Both poll periods are the owner's choice. S7-D1 (owner, 2026-11-17 ~16:10Z, "zgoda") keeps them for every index, the PRN sitemap included; probe10's 5-min period (§11.6) was a probe's, not a plan's.
  - Rejected: a 5-min tick (step-7 ops report S2; its numbers from step-7 critique C7, 2026-11-17). With one store lease, every run gets a window of at most 150 s, and a host answering 5xx with no `Retry-After` is asked 864 to 1,152 times a day, against 202 under the 31 slots (arithmetic 2026-11-17: doublings reset each run; 288 ticks × 3 or 4 requests, against 23 × 6 + 8 × 8).
- Measured 2026-11-16 (Monday) against each index, 26 polls on the table's schedule (§11.6, weekday probe):
  - no overlap = the later poll's oldest item is newer than the earlier poll's newest; the publication time between the two is an uncovered stretch, and an item listed and dropped inside it is never seen.
  - PRN and GNW RSS: no overlap at both periods (table); items lost in the uncovered stretches are not counted, because the index cannot show them.
  - CNW sitemap, CNW RSS and Yahoo: every pair overlapped and kept the previous poll's newest item.
  - Fed: no new item all day, so the period was not tested.

| index | items per fetch | window h, min / median / max | pairs with no overlap: 30-min / 90-min | uncovered stretch per no-overlap pair, min–max | fewest of the previous poll's items still present |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GNW RSS | 20 | 0.15 / 0.4585 / 1.033 | 15 of 22 / 3 of 3 | 1–75 min | 0 of 20, in 18 pairs |
| PRN RSS | 20 | 0.2 / 0.4335 / 0.983 | 15 of 22 / 3 of 3 | 1–50 min | 0 of 20, in 17 pairs |
| CNW sitemap | 100 | 7.1 / 11.375 / 60.95 | 0 of 22 / 0 of 3 | — | 86 of 100 (14:00→14:30, 22:00→23:30) |
| CNW RSS | 20 | 0.867 / 2.225 / 16.183 | 0 of 22 / 0 of 3 | — | 5 of 20 (14:00→14:30) |
| Fed RSS | 20 | 1,176.75, every poll | 0 of 22 / 0 of 3 | — | 20 of 20, every pair |
| Yahoo sitemap | 1,000 | 1.783 / 3.438 / 20.367 | 0 of 22 / 0 of 3 | — | 576 of 1,000 (14:00→14:30) |

  - Uncovered stretch = the later poll's oldest publication value − the earlier poll's newest, minute precision; computed here from the probe's per-poll rows.
  - The true PRN and GNW daily volume is unknown and may be higher than what was seen: first sightings reach 40 in an hour, the most two 20-item polls can show (§11.6's hourly table).
  - The remedy for PRN and GNW is the completeness table below: owner decisions `PG-D2` to `PG-D6` of 2026-11-17 ("tak, zgadzam sie z rekomendacjami", after the PRN/GNW research of that day) and S7-D1 (above). PRN's RSS-plus-sitemap row is built since 2026-11-17 and its PG-D6 row in part: the RSS item is kept, its absence from the sitemap is not recorded (the bullets below the table); the GNW row is built since 2026-11-17 (its bullets below); the PRN archive row is decided, not built: no Rust, rules or hosts file names a PRN archive (`git grep` 2026-11-17).

    | host | what is read | rule | ObservationTime | rejected, and the symptom |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | PRN | the RSS, as today, plus the news sitemap `sitemap-news.xml?page=<n>` | each poll reads page 1, then the next page, until a page shares a key with the basis poll (the newest poll that read every page it planned, a broken boundary included: owner decision 2026-11-17, option B); the poll is complete only when every page boundary it read also shares a key and its last page holds a basis key; otherwise that stretch is recorded "not looked" | the document fetch, as today | the RSS alone: 0 of the 55 releases of 14:00Z on 2026-11-16 seen by the 20-item polls; the sitemap alone: 7 RSS keys of 2026-11-16 are in no sitemap page, 2 of them across a boundary whose pages shared rows; one generation stamp for every page read: 0 of 20 neighbouring page pairs shared one, because Cloudflare caches each page url for 300 s (PRN/GNW research, 2026-11-17, over the 2026-11-16 bodies) |
    | PRN | the monthly archive `Sitemap_Index_<Mon>_<YYYY>.xml.gz` | names what the RSS and the sitemap both missed; an item found only there is stored; unchecked: its size against `BODY_LIMIT` and its `<lastmod>` as publication time (§10's still-open list) | the archive fetch (PG-D5), weeks after publication | — |
    | PRN | — | a release the RSS lists and the sitemap leaves out stays an ordinary RSS item; the absence is recorded as a fact, never as a withdrawal (PG-D6) | — | reading it as withdrawn: why PRN leaves a release out is unknown |
    | GNW | the RSS as the frontier source, plus an id walk on `rss.globenewswire.com` (`/news-release/<y>/<m>/<d>/<id>/0/en/x.html`, probe10's url) | each poll walks ids up to the highest id the PREVIOUS RSS poll showed (PG-D2: a one-period lag by construction, so an id is asked at least one poll period after a higher id was listed; whether an id stays non-public longer, an embargo, is unknown, bounded by probe10 in §11.6; an id asked early answers 410, which GNW's CDN keeps for that url for 30 days); a 301 to another language is recorded "id exists, language X, not fetched" and not followed (PG-D4); a 410 is a permanent answer, never asked again (owner decision 2026-11-17 ~21:30Z); stories keyed `(dc:identifier, language)`; the basis rule is PRN's (owner decision 2026-11-17, option B): every poll that read all the ids it planned is a basis, a gap stays recorded on that poll, and a poll with a planned id not read is no basis | the instant the id fetch returned | GNW's `latest.xml`: left out 143 of 406 English releases and has no time field (research 2026-11-17, not re-checked); following the 301: one more request per non-English id, against `MAX_REDIRECTS` 0 (§10) |

    - `sitemaps.globenewswire.com`'s robots.txt answered 404: read as allow-all (PG-D3), as §10's robots row reads every 404.
    - Built 2026-11-17, PRN's first row:
      - Hosts: the PRN row's optional `paged_index` (`index_url` `https://www.prnewswire.com/sitemap-news.xml?page=1`, `rule` `prn_sitemap`); `hosts/2` stays, since a reader that does not know the field refuses it. `plan` asks page 1 after the RSS; `admitted_paths` adds `/sitemap-news.xml?page=1`, which PRN's pinned robots allows.
        - Rejected: a second hosts row for the host: `DuplicateHost`, the robots and pace maps and the host-to-rule lookup are keyed by host. A `paged` flag on the rule: CNW reads the same sitemap shape, and paging CNW is not decided.
      - Rule `prn_sitemap`: CNW's sitemap rule with PRN's `section.release-body`. A document is read under the rule whose `valid_format` reads its listed value (`OpenWebFeed::rule_of`), so a sitemap follow-up is dated by `news:publication_date` and an RSS one by `pubDate`. `open` refuses a paged index not ending in the query pair `page=1` (`PagedIndexNotPageOne`), read under a rule that is no news sitemap (`PagedIndexNotASitemap`), or whose rule and the row's own read one probe value alike (`IndexFormatsOverlap`).
        - Rejected: a request field naming the index that listed a document: every `Request` literal and the `payloads` row would carry it, while today a stored document is re-read from its url and `listed_date` alone.
        - Hole: a sitemap row with no readable minute is read under the RSS rule, so its unusable row names `pubDate` as its field.
      - One story per release: both rows key through `item_key`. Replayed 2026-11-17 over probe10's 48 RSS and 49 sitemap bodies through the committed feed's `story_of` (a temporary test, not kept): 438 RSS stories, 744 sitemap stories, 417 in both, the counts §11.6 states from the probe's own keys.
      - In one run, a story an earlier index issued counts as held when a later index lists it (`news_provider::collect::attempt_once`), so a release both list is fetched once, from the RSS, which is asked first; its `listed_date` is the RSS's and its ObservationTime is that document fetch's.
      - Paging (`paging::next_page`): page N+1 is an `Index` follow-up carrying, as `listed_date`, the listed value of page N's oldest row, and it is planned only when all hold:
        - a basis was handed (none: `first_poll`, page 1 only);
        - page N holds no basis key;
        - page N's oldest row is not older than the basis's oldest row, a tie going on; pages list newest first: 0 rows newer than the row before them over probe10's 49 pages, 4,900 rows (Python, 2026-11-17);
        - page N's oldest row is not newer than page N−1's, a tie going on;
        - its url is allowed by the host's pinned robots (§11.1's per-document check).
        - Rejected: a tie as a stop: one minute can fill a page of 100 rows; 107 releases were listed at 09:00 ET on 2026-11-17, 98 of them on 14:10Z's page 1 (PR review, probe10, 2026-11-17).
        - No page count is read and no cap is fitted: the walk ends at the basis, past the basis's oldest minute, at a page newer than the one before, or at a page answered empty or not usable.
        - Rejected: the sitemap index `sitemap-news.xml` (19 pages listed on 2026-11-14, 12 at 08:16Z and 23 at 20:26Z on 2026-11-17: research and probe 11, §11.6) as the cap: one more request per poll, and no request carries its count to the page that would need it. A fixed page cap: a fitted number.
        - Past its last page PRN answers 200 with an empty `urlset` (probe 11, §11.6): `Empty`, so the walk ran out and the poll is `no_previous_key`, a basis, and the run exits 0. It is reached only when the basis is older than the sitemap's depth, 70.5 h on 2026-11-17 (research). Test: `a_poll_whose_next_page_is_past_the_end_ran_out_and_is_the_basis_of_the_next`, over the real page-24 body.
      - Continuity (`paging::continuity`, after the run in `cmd_collect::continuity_of`):
        - `first_poll` with no basis;
        - else `next_page_unread` when the last page holds no basis key and `next_page` plans a page after it that was not read usable: it failed, or robots disallow it. It wins over a broken boundary, so a poll that read less than it planned is never a basis.
        - else `boundary_no_shared_key` when two neighbouring pages share no key; else `complete` when the last page holds a basis key.
        - else `no_previous_key`, the walk having run out: no next page was due, or the next page answered `Empty` (`Committer::empty_index_pages`).
        - Recorded on the poll's page-1 attempt (§3.2's `continuity`), in the envelope's `continuity` and counted by the ledger.
      - Generation label (`paging::generations`, owner decision 2026-11-17): each boundary whose two pages share no key gets one `Generation` from both pages' `x-sitemap-generated-at`, read as RFC 3339 instants and compared to the millisecond (`Generation::between`):

        | label | stamps | what it says of the gap |
        |---|---|---|
        | `same_generation` | equal | pages of one generation join exactly, so no row fell between them |
        | `older_generation` | the later page older | a real gap |
        | `newer_generation` | the later page newer | rows may repeat; still no key shared |
        | `generation_unknown` | absent or not RFC 3339 on either page | nothing |

        - The label only describes the gap. It never decides `complete` against a gap, nor the basis; both stay with the shared-key rule. With the header dropped by PRN every label is `generation_unknown` and the rest is unchanged.
        - `Last-Modified` is not read: on 3 of 3 probe-11 responses it is the stamp cut to the second, so two stamps inside one second would read as equal.
        - Stored as four counts beside `continuity` (§3.2's `boundary_*`), in the envelope's `continuity[].boundaries` and on the `continuity` human line, and summed by the ledger (`boundaries`, human `boundary_<label>`).
        - Rejected: labels derived at ledger time from `payloads.headers`: the ledger would re-read and re-parse every page blob under the rules to find which boundaries share no key. One row per boundary in a new table: its page column would have no reader.
      - Basis (`Continuity::is_basis`): the newest poll that read every page it planned, so every continuity but `next_page_unread`, a broken boundary included (owner decision 2026-11-17, option B, "zgadzam się" to the PR #47 review's F1), read before the lease (`store::basis_pages`, blobs by digest; a missing blob is `BlobAbsent` and refuses the run).
        - The cost accepted: a broken boundary's stretch stays recorded on its poll (`boundary_no_shared_key`, labelled) and the sitemap walk never looks for it again; probe10's 13:51–13:59Z stays unread, 14:15Z being `complete` at page 1 against 14:10Z's two pages (§11.6's replay). Its remedy is the monthly archive (PG-D5, a later PR).
        - Rejected: a broken boundary as no basis (PR #47): the next poll walks back to an older basis across more pages and boundaries, each able to break again. Over the research's 12-page pass of 2026-11-17 08:17Z, with the basis at page k = 2..12, all 11 walks were `boundary_no_shared_key`, 2 to 11 pages (§11.6's pass table); once no basis replaces it the walk grows to the sitemap's last page. A broken stretch stored as a minute interval and closed by a later poll: proposed, not chosen (owner decision 2026-11-17).
        - Rejected: complete polls only: an empty store's first poll is never complete, so no poll would ever anchor, and a basis gone from the sitemap would make every later poll walk every page.
        - Rejected: `no_previous_key` whatever ended the walk, an unread page included: 14:10Z with page 2 answering 503 three times became the basis, and 14:15Z read page 1 only and was `complete` while 13:51–13:59Z stayed unread (PR review, a temporary test, 2026-11-17).
        - Unguarded: `complete` assumes a release newer than a row already seen is listed above it. Over probe10 at a 5-min stride, 35 keys first appeared on page 1 below a row an earlier cycle saw; 19 of the 20 printed were dated before that row (cycle 28: 15 rows at 09:00–09:01 ET at rows 17–99, below a row at 09:02) (PR review, stride.py, 2026-11-17). At 5 min all 35 were on page 1; at 30 or 90 min such a row on a page below the one holding the basis key is missed while the poll reads `complete`. Remedy: the monthly archive, a later PR; no page margin is fitted.
        - A run committing between this read and the lease leaves an older basis, which reads more pages, never fewer.
        - `basis_pages` reads a store at user_version 10 to 14, because the run migrates the store after it (`a_v10_to_v14_store_keeps_its_basis_for_the_run_that_migrates_it`). Rejected: this build's version only: the first run after the step to 11 read no basis and recorded `first_poll` (PR #48 review, a temporary test, 2026-11-17).
      - PG-D6: an RSS release no sitemap page lists stays an ordinary RSS item (`a_poll_whose_page_one_overlaps_the_first_poll_is_complete_and_reads_one_page`). Its absence is derivable from the stored RSS and sitemap payloads; no field reports it (not built).
      - The plan check is per (day, url) since 2026-11-17 (the conformance bullet below); a next page is no vendor row of the page that planned it.
      - Not built: the Yahoo cross-check reads PRN's RSS alone as PRN's direct index, so a release only the sitemap listed can still be named unlisted.
      - Probe10 and the research's 12-page pass replayed through the committed code: §11.6's replay tables. Tests and mutations: §11.5 step 6's PRN completeness.
    - Built 2026-11-17, the GNW row (owner decisions PG-D2, PG-D4, option B, and ~21:30Z's option A and 410 rule):
      - Hosts: the GNW row's optional `id_walk`, `url` `https://rss.globenewswire.com/news-release/{date}/{id}/0/en/x.html` (probe10's url); `hosts/2` stays. The walk's language is the url's, `en`.
        - `open` refuses a url that does not expand to one release on the row's host (`IdWalkNotARelease`), and a row whose rule keys no story by release or reads no page time (`IdWalkRuleNotReleaseKeyed`).
      - Rules: `gnw` gains `story_key: release` (§11.1's `item_key` bullets) and `page_valid` (`meta[property="og:article:published_time"]`, `content`, `%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ`); a `page_valid` format that prints no second is refused.
      - Range (`Walk::range`), read before the lease by `cmd_collect::ranges_of` through `store::walk_read`:
        - through: the highest id, any language, of the newest usable answer of the index, the previous poll's frontier (PG-D2); the current poll's own answer never raises it.
        - after: the id the newest basis poll walked through (`attempts.walk_through`); when that basis walked nothing (`first_poll`), the lowest id its own answer lists, less one.
        - none, so `first_poll` and no walk, when the store holds no usable answer of the index or no basis poll; a store below user_version 12 holds no basis, so the first run after the step is a first poll.
        - unknown, so `range_unread` (`WalkPlan::RangeUnread`) and no walk, when the store holds both and the previous answer, or a basis that walked nothing, lists no release on the walked host (a changed link shape): no `walk_through`, no basis, so the next poll walks from the same basis. While the shape stays changed every poll is `range_unread`, counted by the ledger.
          - Rejected: `first_poll` there: the poll became a basis with no `walk_through` and the next poll started below the lowest id of a later answer, never asking the ids between (PR #49 review, a temporary test, 2026-11-18).
        - date: the frontier row's own path date `<y>/<m>/<d>`; GNW answered 200 with the same body for a wrong date (research fetch #11, 2026-11-17).
      - Requests (`OpenWebFeed::walk_of`, `Extracted::walk`): each id of the range as a `Document` with no listed value, leaving out an id the store answered in the walk's language, an id this answer lists in it (its row asks it: one fetch, one story), and a url the host's pinned robots disallow (§11.1's per-document check).
        - The driver issues them after the listed follow-ups, minus held stories and stories issued earlier in the run, outside `matched`, `held` and `relisted` (`news_provider::collect::attempt_once`).
          - The held filter drops a story committed after `open_provider` read the answered ids: `a_walked_id_held_after_the_range_was_read_is_not_asked`.
          - Unguarded: the in-run filter, and walked stories added to the issued set. The feed already leaves out an id its own answer lists and one index per host is walked, so removing either turned no test red; nor did issuing the walk only when the index matched a row (PR #49 review, 2026-11-18).
      - Answered (`store::answered_urls`): a document attempt closed `usable`, `empty` or `permanent_failure`, a 3xx included. A 3xx is not held (§11.1's held table): a listed row is fetched again by the next poll, the walk never asks that id again.
      - A walked page is dated by the minute holding its `og:article:published_time` (`minute_containing`): `valid_raw` keeps the second, `valid_field` is `og:article:published_time`. With no readable time it is `NoParseableValidTime` decided by the page, so held. Its ObservationTime is the instant its fetch returned, as for any live document.
        - 16 of 16 GNW pages' time fell in their RSS `pubDate`'s minute, 4 of them with a second other than 0 (§11.6).
        - Only the walk's own url (`OpenWebFeed::walked`) is dated by its page; a listed url with no listed value stays `NoParseableValidTime` decided by its index row: `a_listed_url_with_no_listed_value_is_not_dated_by_its_page`. Rejected: any document with no listed value: an RSS row with no `pubDate` was dated by its page while one with an unreadable `pubDate` was refused (PR #49 review, 2026-11-18).
      - A 3xx whose `Location` names the same id on the same host in another language is `Empty` (`OpenWebFeed::classify`, `walk::moved_to`): an answer, held, never followed; its language reaches the envelope's `walk[].other_language` from the stored `Location` (§3.9's P9 row). Any other 3xx stays `PermanentFailure` (§10).
        - Rejected: `PermanentFailure`, §11.1's rule for every 3xx: each run walking a non-English id would exit 8; 48 of 466 probe6 RSS links were not English (research 2026-11-17).
        - Rejected: the language parsed from the 3xx body, an "Object moved" page whose shape nothing fixes; a language column on `attempts`, when P9 already keeps the header beside the blob.
      - A walked id answered 410 is `PermanentFailure` like any 4xx: an attempt row, held, never retried, never asked under another slug, counted in `walk_gone`. It is an answer, so its poll stays a basis; the run exits 8 as for any 4xx (`news_provider::Tally::exit`).
      - Continuity (`Walk::continuity`, after the run in `cmd_collect::walk_of`): `first_poll` and `range_unread` as planned; `complete` when every id of the range is answered after the run; else `id_unread`, a transient failure left after its retries or a robots-disallowed url, which is no basis (`Continuity::is_basis`), so the next poll walks from the older basis again.
      - Recorded on the poll's usable index attempt (§3.2's `continuity` and `walk_*`); in the envelope's `walk[]`: index url, attempt, `after`, `through`, `date`, `asked`, `continuity`, and the `unread`, `gone` and `other_language` ids; a `walk` human line; summed by the ledger's `walk` (`complete`, `first_poll`, `id_unread`, `range_unread`, `gone`, `other_language`, `unread`) and its human line.
      - Cap: none of its own (option A): bounded by its range and, once built, by S7-D3's deadline, unreached ids deferred and carried (S7-D4, S7-D5); its ids are outside S7-D6's 150 (§11.5 step 7): `walk_ids_are_never_counted_against_the_cap_or_deferred` (the queue) and `a_walked_poll_past_the_cap_sends_every_walk_id_and_defers_listed_releases_only` (through `collect`), 2026-11-18.
      - Unguarded: a frontier read from one wrong id (a mistyped id, an id of another sequence) plans every id up to it, in memory, and asks them; nothing bounds the range. Probe10's frontier moved at most 70 ids in 5 min (§11.6).
      - No range guard, owner decision 2026-11-18 ("Zgadzam sie"): S7-D3's deadline bounds the walk (built 2026-11-18, §11.2's schedule bullets), a wrong id answers 404, and the ledger's `walk` counts show the range's size.
        - The lower end likewise: a basis that walked nothing starts below the lowest id its answer lists, any language, so one stray low id plans every id above it. Across probe10's 48 GNW polls the widest spread inside one poll was 68 ids, with no outlier (PR #49 review, 2026-11-18).
      - Frontier age by slot, built 2026-11-18 (owner decision 2026-11-18, PR #52 answer 1; §11.5 step 7 PR 5): the range's top is read only from the newest usable answer received before the start of `--at`'s schedule slot (`Schedule::slot_at`, `store::walk_read`), so each poll walks to an answer of an earlier slot: PG-D2's one-slot lag.
        - Exclusive: an answer received at 12:29:59.999999 is read by a 12:30:30 poll, one received at 12:30:00 is not (`the_walks_frontier_answer_must_be_received_before_its_slot_starts`).
        - In real time order, an answer received at 12:00:31 is read by the 12:30:30 poll (`in_real_time_order_a_walk_reads_the_answer_of_the_slot_before`), and not by a manual poll at 12:00:45, which is `range_unread` (`a_manual_poll_in_the_same_slot_does_not_read_that_slots_answer`).
        - The slot is `--at`'s, read when `open_provider` reads the walk, before `run` reads the clock. A live open-web run whose `--at` is after the clock is `invalid_argument` (`AtAfterTheClock`, `an_open_web_run_whose_at_is_after_the_clock_is_refused`), so `--at`'s slot starts no later than the clock's.
        - A run with no slot is refused, `ScheduleUnusable` (`ScheduleRefusal::NoSlotAt`), before any request: `slot_at` reads the day before and after, so it finds none only for an `--at` on chrono's first or last day (`an_at_with_no_schedule_slot_before_it_is_refused_by_name`, `--at` at `DateTime::MIN_UTC`). EDGAR has no walk and reads no frontier.
        - Rejected (PR #53 mutation review M17, 2026-11-18): a cutoff at the earliest instant when no slot was found; no test reached it, so a change to that fallback turned nothing red.
        - Only answers of the run's own slot, and a basis: `range_unread`, no id asked and no basis, so the next poll walks from the same basis; no usable answer at all stays `first_poll`.
        - Rejected: `first_poll` there: the poll would be a basis that walked nothing, and the next poll would start below the lowest id of this poll's answer, never asking the ids between the older basis and it (the PR #49 review's `range_unread` case).
        - Rejected: `--at` less the shortest period, 30 min, PR 4's rule: a live poll's answer is received after its own `--at`, so the next slot's poll found it 29:59 old and the lag was two slots, about 60 min (PR #52 contract and mutation reviews, F1 each).
        - Closes PR #49 review F1: a run 15 s after the previous one asked 3404412 and 3404417, which that answer had listed 15 s earlier; an id asked too early answers 410 and is never asked again.
        - The lag is the gap to the previous slot in `schedule::SLOTS`, a construction; §11.6 bounds the embargo at 10 min or more only:

          | slot of `--at` | previous slot | lag |
          |---|---|---|
          | 11:30 to 22:00 | 30 min earlier | 30 min |
          | 11:00 | 09:30 | 90 min |
          | 23:30 | 22:00 | 90 min |
          | 00:30 | 23:30 | 60 min |
          | 02:00 to 09:30 | 90 min earlier | 90 min |
      - Cost, not measured: `store::answered_urls` reads every answered document url of the source, all hosts, on every run, twice: before the lease and after the run.
      - Unguarded: the walk asks English only; an id listed only in another language costs one request answered 301, and an id with no English page and no listing is known only by that 301.
      - Not built: asking a 410 id again (owner decision 2026-11-17: decided on live data). The carry does not: a 410 is held.
      - Replayed over probe10 at a 30-min stride: §11.6's walk replay. Tests and mutations: §11.5 step 6's GNW walk.
    - Cost, a projection and not a measurement (step-7 numbers report, 2026-11-17, read at main 21989d7: four hosts, Yahoo counted only where a bullet says so, against a 900 s deadline; from probe6's indexes and the research's sitemap bodies; waits only, 10 s per request):
      - GNW: 1,036 ids over 08:00–23:30Z of 2026-11-16 plus 31 RSS requests, 37.05% of a day per host at × 3 (1 + `MAX_TRANSIENT_RETRIES`); a lower bound, the night not counted.
      - PRN: sitemap items per 30 min, each window shifted by the 10.8 min median generation lag, were 66, 75 and 109 at 13:00, 13:30 and 14:00Z on 2026-11-16, and 120 at 13:00Z on 2026-11-17 (from that day's sitemap pages); a page holds 100, so a peak poll needs page 2. PRN's daily share under S7-D1 is not computed.
      - At a 30-min stride over probe10, 6 of 42 page-1 pairs shared no key, so the later poll needed page 2: cycles 17, 18, 19, 22, 26 and 27 (PR review, stride.py, 2026-11-17).
      - Unmeasured at 30 min: how often a multi-page PRN poll is not complete, because Cloudflare serves each page url from a cache of 300 s, so a later page can come from an older generation sharing no key with page 1; probe10's one multi-page read was not complete (§11.6), and 18 of 40 page boundaries in the research had a later page from an older generation (PRN/GNW research, 2026-11-17, counting the copies each lens made of one pair); over the two distinct passes on disk, 10 of 21 neighbouring pairs were `older_generation` and 11 shared a key (PR #48 review, `Paging::generations`, 2026-11-17).
      - Inside one 30-min run, at the 2026-11-16 peak, four hosts: the run reaches 1,170 s at p1430, 1,110 s at p1500 and 880 s at p1600, so S7-D3's 870 s deadline binds at all three; the cap of 150 binds at p1430 (183 follow-ups, 33 cut, PRN's tail).
      - With Yahoo's followed keys added: the cap binds at p1430 (228, 78 cut) and p1500 (203, 53 cut) (numbers report §3), and at p1330 (175, 25 cut) and p1400 (180, 30 cut), derived 2026-11-17 by adding the report's §2 Yahoo column to its §3 follow-ups; Yahoo's lane is at most 58 requests, 570 s (p1500).
    - Measured at a 5-min period, not at S7-D1's 30 min: §11.6's probe10.
  - CNW's admitted index is the sitemap row (§10's CNW table, 2026-11-17); the CNW RSS row is the rejected alternative.
- The stop deadline (the table's third column, S7-D3) is what holds the run's wall time ≤ 50% of a day (arithmetic 2026-11-17):
  - requests stop by 23 × 870 s + 8 × 2,670 s = 41,370 s, 47.9% of 86,400 s;
  - with a last request running its 30 s patience in every run, wall time ≤ 23 × 900 s + 8 × 2,700 s = 42,300 s, 48.96%;
  - this assumes ureq's 30 s `timeout_global` covers the body read on every path, which nobody has tested (step-7 critique, open items).
  - One host asks at most 870 s / 10 s + 1 = 88 requests in a 30-min run (§10's per-host pace inside S7-D3's half-window); measured 86 for PRN in the runs of 2026-11-19 that stopped at 862–867 s.
  - `busy_s` undercounts a host's spacing: `waited_s` counts only that host's own sleep inside `Exchange::reserve`, not the time other hosts' turns take, so the real spacing is ≥ 10 s while PRN's `busy_s` read about 5 s per request on 2026-11-19.
  - Since round-robin by host (§10, 2026-11-17) that wall time is about the busiest host's paced waits plus every request time, not the waits summed over hosts. A `Retry-After` hold that ends by the deadline breaks this: it is waited out on the held host's turn and every other host waits with it (§10 table: 150 s against block order's 130 s; §8 row R19, hole 12); one that ends past it stops the held host and the other hosts keep their turns (2026-11-18).
  - Unissued requests are `deferred` rows (S7-D4), and the next run carries each unresolved document the store does not hold into its host's lane, behind every request of this run on that host, closing it `expired` after 24 h (S7-D5); built 2026-11-18, §3.2's `deferred_closed`.
  - Built 2026-11-18: the schedule bullets above. `deferred` is built since 2026-11-18, with `follow_up_cap`, `run_deadline` and `host_held` as its producers (§3.2).
  - Carried, not left to re-listing: PRN's and GNW's next poll shared no item with the previous one in 18 of 25 pairs each (table above).
- Follow-up cap: 150 per invocation, the owner's choice, not measured; global, counted at issue in round-robin order, and the GNW walk's ids are outside it, an explicit exception (owner decision 2026-11-17 ~21:30Z, option A; S7-D6, §11.5 step 7). Worst case per run with no host slowing itself (§11.1 Pace): (150 + index requests) × (1 + `MAX_TRANSIENT_RETRIES` = 2) × 10 s.
  - Built 2026-11-18 (§11.5 step 7 PR 2): `open_web_feed::FOLLOW_UP_CAP` = 150 through `Provider::follow_up_cap`; `edgar-press` declares `None` (`edgar_press_declares_no_follow_up_cap`), because a full EDGAR day asked 502 documents on 2026-11-13 at its own 100 ms pace and its archive arm converges by §8 row R20.
  - Counted: a `Document` follow-up an index listed, once, when `HostQueue::pop` hands it out (`Queued::counted`). Only the collect unit tests tell counting at issue from counting at completion: the driver asks one request at a time, so through `collect` the two give one result (mutation 2026-11-18, the count moved after `attempt_once`: the six unit tests red, the integration tests green). Not counted: a planned request, an `Index` follow-up (PRN's next sitemap page), a walk id (`Extracted::walk`) and a retry (§8 row R21).
    - An `Index` follow-up is outside the 150 because the worst case above already counts index requests apart from it. Rejected: counting it; a cut page 2 leaves its poll `next_page_unread`, no basis, so the next poll cannot join either (§11.2's PRN completeness row).
    - A retry asks the same follow-up again, so it is never a new one. Rejected: counting each retry; a host answering 503 would spend up to 3 of the 150 per follow-up and push other hosts' follow-ups past the cap.
  - The 151st counted follow-up, and every one after it, is a `deferred` row with reason `follow_up_cap` and is never sent (§3.2); the run exits 8 `partial_collect` (S7-D8, §5.1).
  - First run on an empty store, a projection (arithmetic 2026-11-18, the queue simulated in Python: one lane per host, round-robin, the cap counted at pop; PRN's sitemap index takes one turn before its follow-ups):
    - inputs: GNW RSS 20, PRN sitemap page 1 100 plus 0 to 20 RSS-only releases (in-run dedup, `issued`), CNW 100, the Fed 20 (§11.2's index sizes, 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-16), Yahoo 141 followed rows at p1500 (2026-11-16); a first poll walks no GNW id (`first_poll`).

      | case | follow-ups | deferred | PRN | CNW | Yahoo |
      |---|---|---|---|---|---|
      | five hosts, Yahoo at p1500 | 381 to 401 | 231 to 251 | 64 to 84 | 63 | 104 |
      | four hosts, no Yahoo key row | 240 to 260 | 90 to 110 | 45 to 65 | 45 | — |
      | S7-D6's row: PRN by its RSS alone, 20 | 301 | 151 | 0 | 55 | 96 |

    - GNW and the Fed lose nothing in each case: their 20 are issued within the first 20 rounds.
    - The last row is reproduced by `news_provider::collect::tests::the_global_cap_cuts_55_of_cnws_and_96_of_yahoos_first_run_follow_ups`; the other two rows are not tested.
    - The next run carries each deferred story (S7-D5, §3.2's `deferred_closed`).
  - A story is added to the run's `issued` set when its index is read, before the queue decides to ask or defer it, so a second index of the same run listing a deferred story (PRN's sitemap beside its RSS) drops it and writes no second `deferred` row; since 2026-11-18 it counts `held` only once the run asked it (§3.2's `held`).
  - Counted too since 2026-11-18: a carried follow-up that is no walk id (S7-D5, §3.2's `deferred_closed`).
  - Busy time against the stop deadline and this cap is projected, not measured, and a lower bound: the completeness cost bullets above (round-robin, 2026-11-17); §11.6's weekday probe busy-time table (2026-11-16) is the serial sum from before round-robin.
- Conformance: exempt by a provider declaration, a new trait method without a default; `edgar-press` declares itself session-bound, so its check stays.
  - Built 2026-11-15: `Provider::cadence()` returns `Cadence::{SessionBound, EveryUtcDay}`; `news_provider::collect` refuses a calendar or listing the cadence does not take, and a missing one it needs, with `PlanRefusal::InputsDoNotMatchCadence` before the startup sweep reads the archive; `--dry-run` refuses the same way, untested. No CLI input reaches either refusal: `cmd_collect::open_provider` refuses the same mismatch first, from the flags, before the store is read (below). `OpenWebFeed::plan` runs the same check itself, so a future driver that skips both still gets the refusal: `a_calendar_or_a_listing_handed_to_plan_is_refused` (review 2026-11-15: the check removed, every test stayed green before this test; mutation that day: red).
  - Tests, each turned red by one mutation 2026-11-15: `edgar_press_declares_itself_session_bound` (declaration set to `EveryUtcDay`); `an_every_utc_day_provider_is_not_held_to_the_listed_session_check` (check run unconditionally); `a_session_bound_provider_that_asks_for_a_closed_day_is_still_named` (check never run); `a_session_bound_run_with_no_calendar_is_refused_before_any_request` (driver check removed: the archive is read first).
- `collect` takes `--calendar` and `--listing` per provider; the open-web run takes neither. Accounting is per (day, url) since 2026-11-17, per (day, host) before.
  - Built 2026-11-15 in `cmd_collect::open_provider` over the table `cmd_collect::Registered` (source, credential name, contact variable, own files, cadence):

| flag | `edgar_press` | `open_web_wires` | decided by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--calendar`, `--listing` | required | refused | `Registered::cadence`, equal to the opened provider's `Cadence` (`every_registered_cadence_is_the_one_its_provider_declares`; mutation 2026-11-15, the open-web entry `SessionBound`: red) |
| `--roster` | required | refused | the table |
| `--hosts` | refused | required | the table |
| `--lag-policy` | optional | refused (live only) | `open_provider` |
| `--rules` | required: the chosen provider's file | same | clap |
| contact variable, or the file its `_FILE` twin names (2026-11-18) | `BELLWETHER_SEC_CONTACT` | `BELLWETHER_FEED_CONTACT` | the table |

  - A missing required flag or a refused one is `invalid_argument`, exit 2, before any input file, store read, write or send: `a_flag_error_beside_an_absent_input_file_is_still_a_usage_error` (mutation 2026-11-15, the calendar/listing checks after the provider's `open`: red; review 2026-11-15 measured exit 4 there before the move); `an_open_web_run_refuses_a_calendar_or_listing_flag`, `an_edgar_run_without_a_calendar_is_a_usage_error` (mutation 2026-11-15, the cadence check dropped: both red; the roster/hosts check dropped: the second red; the lag-policy check dropped: the first red); `an_open_web_run_reads_its_contact_from_its_own_variable` (mutation 2026-11-15, both entries on the SEC variable: red).
  - `--window today` is the UTC date of `--at` (`cmd_collect::target_of`); the envelope's `window` is the resolved day: `window_today_resolves_to_the_utc_date_of_at`, run under `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati` with `--at` 23:30Z (mutation 2026-11-15, `chrono::Local`'s date: red).
  - `--window today` for a `SessionBound` source (`edgar_press`) is `invalid_argument`, in `open_provider`, dry run included: a UTC date is not a New York session day. Before 2026-11-15 it was accepted: review that day measured `--at 2026-11-11T02:00:00Z --window today --dry-run` plan `2026-11-11`, exit 0. `an_edgar_run_refuses_window_today` (mutation 2026-11-15, the check removed: red, and the `today for edgar` row of `every_collect_usage_error_is_written_as_a_usage_error`: red).
  - An `EveryUtcDay` run (not a dry run) whose `--at` is not the clock's UTC day is `invalid_argument`, before the envelope is written: `an_open_web_run_whose_at_is_not_the_clocks_day_is_refused_before_anything_is_written` (mutation 2026-11-15, check removed: red). A dry run may name another day.
  - A dry run plans one index request per admitted index, PRN's sitemap page 1 included since 2026-11-17, and writes nothing: `an_open_web_dry_run_plans_one_request_per_admitted_index_and_writes_nothing`.
  - Unguarded: the `required_for` / `refused_for` lists in `newsvault schema` are written by hand beside the table; `every_option_the_collect_parser_takes_is_in_the_machine_readable_schema` checks the names, not those lists.
  - Built 2026-11-15 in `plan_accounts_for_every_day`: a (day, url) pair is asked at most once (a (day, host) pair until 2026-11-17, when PRN's RSS and sitemap became two urls of one host on one day); each day the window names has at least one request or exactly one `NotPlanned` row, never both; a request whose url host `HostKey` refuses is a breach. Host = `news_provider::budget::host_of`, the rule `Wire`'s host pace also uses.
    - Exemption: a `NotPlanned` row naming the whole target window accounts for it only when it is the plan's one row and the plan has no request; otherwise every clause above runs. `a_one_day_target_asked_twice_and_also_not_planned_is_named_and_its_row_alone_is_not` (one-day target `2026-11-14`, the open-web shape; mutation 2026-11-15, the exemption for any whole-window row restored: red; a review probe that day showed that exemption hid a day asked twice and also not planned).
  - Tests: `two_hosts_on_one_day_account_once_each` (red when keyed by day only, 2026-11-15); `two_index_urls_of_one_host_on_one_day_account_once_each` (mutation 2026-11-17, keyed by host again: red); `a_next_index_page_is_not_counted_as_a_vendor_row` (mutation 2026-11-17, every follow-up counted as a row: red); `each_claim_of_the_harness_is_raised_by_a_provider_that_breaks_only_that_claim` gained four arms (one pair asked twice, an unkeyable host, a day both asked and not planned, a day not planned twice), each red under the mutation that drops its clause, 2026-11-15.

- Scheduler: systemd user timers on the VPS running the image, outside the crate (S7-D8 to S7-D10, §11.5 step 7 PR 6): written in `deploy/systemd/`, not installed.

### 11.3 Target shape

| item | target |
|---|---|
| sources | every candidate that passes robots and a weekday probe, incl. Business Wire if stable (its Earnings feed on 2026-11-16: §11.6; no stability threshold is fixed), Zero Hedge, Newswire.com (measured zone, date-only valid time) |
| `open_web_outlets` | Fool, CNBC, Yahoo outlet syndication, Reuters and AFP through Yahoo (owner decision 2026-11-16); Fool's earnings-call transcripts are a separate item kind, field named when built; Business Insider is not added for now (owner decision j, 2026-11-16) |
| per-host intervals | R19 hole 7 closed (`HostPaces::Listed`); the path for a host whose Crawl-delay is above 10 s, refused until then (owner decision 1, 2026-11-16) |
| redirects | followed as their own paced, ledgered requests; today `Wire` follows none for any provider (open-web since 2026-11-15, edgar since 2026-11-16), which closed R19 hole 9 by refusing; `Location` kept since 2026-11-17 (§3.9's P9 row); needs `Document`-phase follow-ups |
| robots | re-fetched on a schedule; a changed robots digest blocks the host until the hosts file is updated. Built 2026-11-15 without the schedule and wider than a host: `robots_refused` refuses the whole run (§5.1) |
| collectors | per SourceId in parallel; needs a startup sweep that tells live runs from dead ones; then the lease covers only `Committer::reserve` through COMMIT (§6) |
| follow-up cap, run length | run length: S7-D3's construction; cap: 150, the owner's choice (S7-D6), built 2026-11-18, to be revisited against measured weekday busy time; busy seconds in every collect envelope and run file since 2026-11-18 (S7-D11), summed per day by the ledger; no live run has measured them, so only a projection exists (§11.2's cap bullet) |
| PRN and GNW completeness | every release named per poll (PRN sitemap pages, built 2026-11-17) or per id (GNW walk, built 2026-11-17), and per month (PRN archive, not built): §11.2's completeness table |
| `NoArticleBody` share per host | a ledger reading; its threshold is fixed before its first measurement |
| outbox poller | wakes `derive` and feeds the private stream (memo D-7) |

### 11.4 Gates planned (each leaves this table for the §8 row named when it bites; none of the rows below exists for the hosts it names, 2026-11-16)

| gate | turns red when | §8 row |
|---|---|---|
| a rolled-back `record` leaves no outbox row | the insert moves outside the transaction | new row O1 |
| a zero-item attempt writes one outbox row | the insert is skipped when rows are empty | R14 |
| CNBC valid time is the page meta (Target) | the sitemap value is used | R13 |

- Unguarded in the POC: no reader of the stored raw valid-time fields (§3.1); items lost between polls (an index whose oldest item is newer than the previous poll's newest): §8 row R16.

### 11.5 Build order (each PR passes the gate alone)

1. Crate skeleton with `[lints] workspace = true`, so row G1 covers it. Built with no dependency and no item; each mutation of the new crate turned the named tests red, 2026-11-15:
   - `[lints]` stanza deleted: `every_workspace_member_inherits_the_lint_floor` (G1)
   - `ureq` added: `only_the_seam_crate_declares_an_http_client` and `the_seam_crate_declares_the_only_client_this_workspace_holds` (R4)
   - a `Utc::now` read added: `no_provider_and_no_part_of_the_seam_outside_wire_and_clock_reads_a_clock` (R4)
2. GNW end to end, the largest PR (touches `pit`, `canon`, `news-provider`, `newsvault` and the new crate): hosts loader, provider table in `cmd_collect` (replacing the single `EdgarPress`); index and document parsers, `item_key`, `Provider::story_of`, `NoArticleBody`; schema versioning, `items` valid-time columns, `hosts_version` column and envelope field; `Minute` precision; conformance declaration and per-(day, host) accounting in `plan_accounts_for_every_day`; per-provider `--calendar`/`--listing`, `--window today`; an end-to-end test.
   - Built 2026-11-15: schema versioning at user_version 1, `items.valid_raw`/`valid_field`/`valid_source`, `attempts.hosts_version` (§3.1, §3.2); `Minute` precision through `pit`, `canon`, `news-provider` and the store, with the `items` rebuild (§3.1, §8 row M1); the conformance declaration and per-(day, host) accounting (§11.2); `Provider::story_of` on the trait, read by `edgar-press`'s `follow_ups_to_issue` (§8 row R20); `UnusableReason::NoArticleBody` ("the article container is absent or yields zero paragraph text"); the `open-web-feed` provider with its hosts and rules loaders, GNW index and document parsers, `item_key`, `NotTheRunDay` and committed fixtures (§11.1), the producer of a non-`None` `Request.hosts_version`, `Published::AtMinute`, `NoArticleBody` and `Cadence::EveryUtcDay`; the R4 no-sink scan (§8). The provider table in `cmd_collect`, per-provider `--calendar`/`--listing`/`--hosts`, `--window today` and the envelope field (§11.2); `the_run_envelope_names_the_hosts_file_it_was_allowed_to_use` drives `cmd_collect::run` over the synthetic index and page (1 item committed).

   - Holes step 6 leaves open for the Fed:
     - one page template seen (9 files, 5 URLs, all `div#article` with a heading block and one release column); another template is `NoArticleBody` or `ArticleContainerAmbiguous`, decided by the page, so held and never fetched again.
     - the lag from a `pubDate` minute to the feed listing it is one point, not a distribution: all 26 polls of 2026-11-16 answered `Last-Modified: Fri, 13 Nov 2026 21:15:18 GMT` (one ETag, one body sha256, Cloudflare `HIT`), 18 s after the newest item's `pubDate` 21:15:00 GMT (bcreg20261113a). `Last-Modified` is when the file changed, not when a client could first read it. No new item was listed that day; the 20 items span 2026-09-25 to 2026-11-13, so a release stays listed for weeks (window 1,176.75 h, §11.2).
     - a `pubDate` with a zero-padded day is `NoParseableValidTime` and fetched by every poll while listed; never observed (0 of 9 single-digit days, 2026-11-16).
     - release text that names a person is stored as it stands: enforcement20261112a names the individual the action is against (P3; r removes addresses only). Media contacts are `div` cells and are not read; a release that puts them in a `p` would store names and numbers, as on PRN and CNW (decision r).
     - the Fed depends on every other admitted robots row and they on its: one unverified row refuses the whole run (§8 row R4), as for PRN and CNW. Its row needs a ledgered 404; a robots.txt that appears later answers 200, which differs from `absent`, so the refresh exits 1 and `collect` refuses the Fed row until the hosts file is edited.
     - no run against the live host yet; the 404 is the 2026-11-16 08:04:29Z answer, not fetched again.
   - Built 2026-11-17: Yahoo, approved by the owner 2026-11-17 ("tak, ruszaj z 1 i 2"). The `yahoo` rule with `provider_keys` and the fifth hosts row (§11.1, §11.2), the sitemap reader's `image:loc` and `news:title`, the cross-check (§11.2, option C) with its store read (`Committer::index_payloads`) and envelope field, its fixtures (§11.2), and the five-host tests. Every default: §10's Yahoo table.

   - Holes step 6 leaves open for Yahoo:
     - one followed page seen, Laverock (`business-wire.com`); no `accesswire.ca` page was fetched, so its template and container are untested. The container was read on 15 pages of other keys (§11.1).
     - Yahoo's own reprint is stored, not the wire's release: the body adds Yahoo's captions and drops nothing measured on GNW, while PRN and CNW reprints lose the `SOURCE` trailer and CNW's contact block (§11.6). No Business Wire or Accesswire original was compared.
     - the path filter is `/`; Yahoo's `*` group has 32 `Disallow` lines below `/` (`/m/`, `/p/`, `/quote/*/earnings/*.html`, …). Closed 2026-11-17 by the per-document check (step 7): a followed row under one is not asked and is counted `robots_disallowed`. No followed row of the 26 polls of 2026-11-16 was under one (2,017 rows, 488 urls; §11.1's re-check).
     - a Yahoo url is not a stable identity: a retitled or moved row is a new key, so a new story and a second fetch (§11.6). Measured 2026-11-17 over the 26 polls of 2026-11-16 (Python): 488 followed urls carry 486 (key, title) pairs; 2 `business-wire.com` releases (Xsolla Clubhouse; the Pierre & Vacances correction) are each under 2 urls, so each would be stored twice.
     - a key is matched exactly: `business-wire.com` spelled otherwise, or a BW release Yahoo files under another key or none, is not followed and is counted only in `filtered_out` and the `cross_check` rows.
     - the index sizes now sum to 301 items a poll at most (20 + 20 + 100 + 20 + 141 followed Yahoo rows at p1500), above step 7's follow-up cap of 150, built 2026-11-18 (§11.2's cap bullet); new followed Yahoo rows were at most 57 a poll (§11.2).
     - a followed page near `BODY_LIMIT` is a `PermanentFailure`, stored cut: Yahoo pages measured 0.93–2.09 MB, Baidu Q3 (`prnewswire.com`) 10,584 B under the limit (§11.1).
     - the cross-check is in the collect envelope only: the ledger does not read it, so a run whose stdout is lost loses it; it is recomputable from the stored blobs. Its title match has the false readings listed in §11.2.
     - a row is `not_looked` while its key's own index is down, even when another index lists it: a `prnewswire.com` row CNW lists (ELEGRP, 2026-11-16) is not compared while PRN does not answer.
     - a Yahoo minute more than 90 min after the direct listing names a listed row unlisted; none of 4,042 matched row-polls of 2026-11-16 was later than 72 min.
     - `LONGEST_POLL_PERIOD` is a copy in `open-web-feed`, held to `schedule::SLOTS`' longest period since 2026-11-18 (`the_cross_check_lead_is_the_committed_schedules_longest_period`; mutation 2026-11-18, 89 min: red).
     - Yahoo depends on every other admitted robots row and they on its (§8 row R4), as for the other hosts.
     - no run against the live host yet; the pin is the 2026-11-16 08:04:29Z body, not re-fetched.

     - Holes:
       - no run against the live host; at S7-D1's 30 min, how often a poll is not complete is unmeasured, and how often it needs page 2 is measured on probe10 only (§11.2's cost bullets).
       - a broken boundary's stretch, left to the monthly archive (not built), and a release listed below a row already seen: §11.2's basis bullets.
       - the label reaches the store through the stub `Exchange`'s header; no live PRN answer has carried the header through `Wire` into the store (no live run). CNW sends it too (probe 11), but CNW is not paged, so no CNW label is produced.
       - `next_page_unread` is reached through a 503 page 2 (`a_poll_whose_page_two_failed_…`); its robots-disallowed arm through `paging::continuity` reading `next_page`, which does not check robots, so no test drives a disallowed page 2 through `collect`.
       - `poll_pages` takes a poll's pages 1, 2, … while each is there; no test reaches a run whose page 2 answered and whose page 1 did not, which cannot happen since page 2 is planned only from page 1's answer.
       - the basis is read before the lease (§11.2's basis bullet), and a basis blob missing from the store refuses the run with no test of its own.
   - GNW walk, §11.2's GNW row, built 2026-11-17:
     - Fixtures in `providers/open-web-feed/tests/fixtures/gnw/`, from probe10's raw bodies (§11.6) unless named otherwise: `rssfeed_1200_4_items.xml` (12:00Z, items 3404422 en, 3404419 en, 3404417 he, 3404415 en, byte for byte), `rssfeed_1200_2_items.xml` (12:00Z, 3404422 and 3404421), `rssfeed_1205_3_items.xml` (12:05Z, 3404462 en and fr, 3404424 en); `walked_3404423.html` and `walked_3404438.html`, walked pages cut to their head lines the rules read and the first 3 container `<p>`; `moved_3403200.html`, the research's 301 body verbatim (fetch #9, CRLF kept), its `Location` a test constant. Hosts fixtures `gnw_walked.json` (the GNW row with its walk, alone) and one per refusal; rules fixtures `rules_story_key_unknown.json`, `rules_page_valid_reads_no_second.json`. No person is named: the feeds' only addresses are GNW's newsdesk and webmaster.

     - Holes:
       - no run against the live host; how walked ids answer is known for probe10's 13 ids (200) and the research's 15 fetches only.
       - `Location` reaches the walk's language only through the stub; the real `Wire` keeps it (`wire` test), unexercised against GNW.
       - a frontier from one wrong id: §11.2's GNW unguarded bullets; a run given `--deadline` defers the walk ids past it as `run_deadline` (`walk_ids_the_deadline_cuts_are_deferred_and_the_walk_is_not_complete`, 2026-11-18); since 2026-11-18 every open-web run with no `--deadline` has the schedule's deadline (§11.2), so no such walk runs past it; a given `--deadline` may be later.
       - the one-period lag, the driver's in-run filter and the `answered_urls` cost: §11.2's GNW bullets.
       - `moved_language`'s store-read error arm: no test makes `payload_of` or a stored header text fail after the commit.
       - `answered_urls`' `closed_at IS NOT NULL` is not reached alone: `verdict` and `closed_at` are written by one `UPDATE` (`store.rs`, the close), so an open attempt has no verdict and the `verdict IN` clause already leaves it out.
       - `minute_containing` refuses a fractional second only through `Minute::new`; the committed `page_valid` format reads no fraction.
7. VPS timer, follow-up cap with the run deadline, busy seconds in the envelope.
   - Hard gates before the first live run; the robots one built 2026-11-17, condition A's core 2026-11-18 (PR 3), its deadline computed from §11.2's schedule since 2026-11-18 (PR 4), S7-D5's carry 2026-11-18 (PR 5):
     - Owner condition A (2026-11-16, "zgadzam sie na warunek A"): step 7 MUST build the run-window cap before the first live run. A host whose `Retry-After`, or doubled wait, ends past the run window is stopped, and its remaining planned requests are recorded as not looked.
       - Built 2026-11-18: `newsvault collect --deadline <RFC 3339>`, after `--at` and, on a run that is not `--dry-run`, after the clock's instant (S7-D2: `--at` may lie in the past), or `invalid_argument` (exit 2: `a_deadline_not_after_at_is_a_usage_error_and_a_later_one_reaches_the_envelope`, `a_deadline_the_clock_has_reached_is_refused_even_after_at`); absent, every request is asked as before. `collect` asks `Exchange::reserve` before `AttemptSlot::open`; a slot opening after the deadline answers `Reservation::PastDeadline { go_at, by_server }` with no attempt row, no pace or hold stamp, no wait and no ordinal; that request and the rest of its host's lane, walk ids and `Index` pages included, are `deferred` rows; other hosts keep their turns, and each reaches its own stop when the deadline passes for it, a url no host keys included.
       - A counted follow-up stopped at the deadline gives its cap slot back (`HostQueue::stop`), since it is never asked: `collect::tests::a_follow_up_stopped_at_the_deadline_spends_no_cap_slot`. Before, with GNW stopped on its first follow-up under a cap of 2, PRN's second was deferred `follow_up_cap` after one follow-up asked (review probe 2026-11-18).
       - The reason split: `host_held` when the profile's own `per_host` from the host's last grant opens by the deadline, so a hold or a doubling moved the slot past it; `run_deadline` otherwise, a hold or a doubling included (`a_slot_the_profiles_own_pace_opens_after_the_deadline_is_run_deadline`); at the boundary, the profile's own slot opening exactly at the deadline, it is `host_held` (`a_hold_past_a_deadline_the_profiles_own_pace_meets_exactly_is_host_held`). Rejected: `host_held` whenever a hold or doubling is in force: a deadline the run's own pace already missed would raise S7-D8's `host_held` alert for a cut that was not the server's.
       - The deadline is inclusive: a slot opening at it is asked, one opening 1 µs after it is not (`a_slot_opening_exactly_at_the_deadline_is_asked_and_one_microsecond_later_is_not`).
     - A per-document robots check, owner decision 2026-11-17 (~16:10Z, "zgoda"): each document url's path is checked against its host's pinned robots before it is asked, read as robots decision 4 (2026-11-17) reads a path (§11.1). Trigger: Yahoo's `path_prefix` is `/`, so collect checked `/` while Yahoo's `*` group has 32 `Disallow` lines below it (step 6's Yahoo holes).
