   - Since 2026-11-18 a `Retry-After` is held at most 24 h in `pace/<host>.hold` and a run given `--deadline` stops the held host (§8 row R19 holes 11 and 12); with no deadline a hold up to 24 h is still waited out in full; the 5xx doubling lives in `Wire::backoff` memory and restarts at 10 s each run (step-7 critique C3, C4, read at main 21989d7, 2026-11-17).
   - Owner decisions S7-D2 to S7-D11, 2026-11-17 (~16:10Z, "zgoda" to the step-7 critique's picks; S7-D5 and S7-D7 at 24 h, the example offered). S7-D1 stands in §11.2. Built: S7-D4 and S7-D6 (PR 2, 2026-11-18); S7-D4's other two reasons, S7-D7 and S7-D8's exit (PR 3, 2026-11-18). S7-D3, S7-D9's run file and ledger half, and S7-D11 (PR 4, 2026-11-18). S7-D5, and the `expired` count and `--check` finding S7-D8's watchdog alerts on (PR 5, 2026-11-18). S7-D8's watchdog, S7-D9's systemd half, S7-D10 as option C, and S7-D5's lane order (PR 6, 2026-11-18): written and validated, not installed on the VPS. The collect's exit metric, one pinned toolchain and N1's daily Nextcloud copy (PR 7, 2026-11-18): the same.

     | decision | rule | rejected, and the symptom |
     |---|---|---|
     | S7-D2, what the deadline measures | the run's wall time | busy time summed over hosts: cuts runs about 4× earlier; a deadline per host: a run can still hold the lease when the next slot starts, giving exit 7 and an unfinished run file |
     | S7-D3, the deadline | `min(slot_start + period/2, next_slot) − VENDOR_PATIENCE` (open-web's, 30 s, `providers/open-web-feed/src/lib.rs`), the slot read from the schedule as data (§11.2's table); built 2026-11-18, §11.2's schedule bullets | process start + period/2: 50.03% of a day in the worst case (31 runs with one last request running its 30 s), and a catch-up start more than period/2 late (more than 15 min in a 30-min slot) runs into the next slot |
     | S7-D4, "not looked" | a `deferred` table (run, host, url, phase, story_id, listed_date, reason in `run_deadline`, `host_held`, `follow_up_cap`) plus a ledger count | an attempt row with no call: breaks §8 row R16, one attempt row per HTTP call; the envelope only: a JSON file cannot feed S7-D5, and "nothing happened" and "we did not look" must differ in the store |
     | S7-D5, deferred items | carried: the next run puts each unresolved deferred item the store does not hold into its host's lane, behind every request of this run on that host, oldest `deferred_at` first (fresh-first, owner decision 1, 2026-11-19); one older than 24 h is closed `expired` | re-listing only: PRN's and GNW's next poll shared no item in 18 of 25 pairs (§11.2); the front of the lane, ahead of the index (as PR 5 built it): a missed index loses a whole period's listing and the GNW frontier; behind the index and ahead of its follow-ups (owner decision 2026-11-18, "tak"): run `open_web-20261119T143004Z` fetched 0 fresh PRN stories while the cap of 150 went to Yahoo 61 + GNW 20 + PRN carried 69, and 155 PRN listings were deferred `follow_up_cap`; promotion of a carried item near its 24 h expiry: no measured path reaches it |
     | S7-D6, the follow-up cap | 150, global, counted at issue in round-robin order; an explicit exception (owner decision 2026-11-17 ~21:30Z, "tak", option A): the GNW walk's ids are outside the 150 and the walk has no cap of its own, bounded by its id range and by S7-D3's deadline (unreached ids `deferred`, S7-D4, and carried, S7-D5), because at the US peak the walk needs about 52–72 requests per 30-min poll (probe10: frontier +566 ids in 3 h 55 min) and round-robin by host already keeps GNW from starving the other hosts; §11.6's 30-min replay walked up to 113 ids in one poll | ⌊150/5⌋ = 30 per host: on a first run's 301 follow-ups (20 + 20 + 100 + 20 + 141 Yahoo rows at p1500) CNW loses 70 and Yahoo 111, against 55 and 96 under the global cap (arithmetic 2026-11-17; the critique's four-host case, before Yahoo: 37 per host, CNW loses 63 against 10) |
     | S7-D7, a server's hold | persisted in the pace file, capped at 24 h, with an alert above it; the pace file then holds a grant no call used, the opposite of §10's rejected "grant earlier than the call" | memory only: asks a held host again every run; uncapped: `Retry-After: 315360000` silences the host for 10 years |
     | S7-D8, the exit | every reason exits 8 `partial_collect` (§5.1); the collect unit sets `SuccessExitStatus=8`, so a failed unit means newsvault's 2, 4 or 7, the store lock's 7, the NTP check's 1, or docker's 125 (a missing image, a container left holding the name); its `ExecStopPost` writes the unit's result and exit status, and `NewsvaultCollectFailed` fires at the next rule evaluation (PR 7; with no rule reading the unit, a failed unit was seen as `NewsvaultCollectStale` 3 to 4 h later or `NewsvaultRunsMissed` the next day, PR #54 review, 2026-11-18); an hourly `ledger --check` watchdog alerts on `host_held` and `expired` counts; built in PR 6 as `ledger --format prometheus` textfile metrics and the rules in `deploy/prometheus/newsvault.rules.yml` (bullets below) | exit 0 for a cut: hides a lost tail; exit 8 as a unit failure: the unit would read failed on most peak runs |
     | S7-D9, overlap | a run refused `lease_held` rewrites its run file with `finished_at` and `refused: lease_held`, and the ledger leaves it out of observed runs; no `Restart=`; `TimeoutStartSec` = the longest deadline + 2 min; built 2026-11-18 but the systemd half: `cmd_collect::run` and the ledger's `runs_lease_held`, whose run still counts toward its day's `runs_expected` (`a_run_the_lease_kept_out_finishes_its_run_file_refused_lease_held`, `the_ledger_sums_busy_time_against_half_a_day_and_leaves_lease_held_runs_unobserved`, whose lease-held file alone states 3 expected runs); a lease-held run whose run id already has a run file puts that file back byte for byte and writes no mark (`a_lease_held_run_puts_back_the_run_file_its_run_id_already_had`; PR #52 mutation review F3: a finished run's file was rewritten `lease_held` and left the ledger's observed runs); the systemd half built in PR 6: no `Restart=`, `TimeoutStartSec=2790` derived from `schedule.rs` (§11.2's longest-window bullet), and a timed-out run's container removed by `ExecStopPost` | skipping the run file: breaks `cmd_collect.rs`'s rule that it is written before the lease, so a run that never gets one is a record |
     | S7-D10, deployment (option C, owner 2026-11-17 ~19:00Z) | CI builds an OCI image from a version tag `v*` and pushes it to GHCR; systemd user timers run it with `docker run --rm --name newsvault-collect`, the fixed name refusing a second container while an orphan stands; the store, pace files included, a bind-mounted volume; the contact a read-only mounted file named by `BELLWETHER_FEED_CONTACT_FILE`; the image named by tag and digest in `~/.config/bellwether/newsvault.env`; an NTP check before each run; a manual first `robots refresh` before go-live; no `RandomizedDelaySec`; a Kubernetes CronJob manifest kept in the repo and validated, not deployed; the VPS store is the first copy (N1, §10) | build on the VPS from a tagged clean clone and run the tests there: rustup, `libsqlite3-dev` and a compile beside AlphaLens on the VPS, and a later move to Kubernetes (the owner's plan) needs an image anyway; `/etc/bellwether/env` mode 640 holding the contact: `docker inspect` prints an environment value |
     | S7-D11, busy seconds | an additive `busy` object in `newsvault.collect/2`, per host: waited, requesting, requests, stopped, not_looked, held_until, doublings, plus `wall_s`; `wall_s` and per-host `busy_s` in `runs/<id>.json`, summed by the ledger against 50%; built 2026-11-18, the bullets below | stdout only: nothing on a real path reads it |

     - S7-D11, built 2026-11-18 (PR 4):
       - Envelope `busy`, null for a dry run: `wall_s`, and per host `host`, `waited_s`, `requesting_s`, `busy_s`, `requests`, `stopped`, `not_looked`, `held_until`, `doublings`; the owner's names, with `_s` on the durations as `wall_s` has it; one `busy` human line and one line per host.
       - `waited_s`: the clock inside `Exchange::reserve`, a stopped reservation's included (`news_provider::collect`).
       - `requesting_s`: each answer's own `received_at`, or `failed_at`, less its `requested_at`, read by `Wire` either side of the socket.
       - `busy_s` = `waited_s` + `requesting_s` (§11.1 Pace); `wall_s` = the clock at `cmd_collect::run`'s start to the run file's `finished_at` (`the_slot_and_the_wall_time_come_from_the_clock_not_from_at`).
         - Not counted: `open_provider`'s reads before `run` (basis, walk, `answered_urls`), and the run file's `started_at` is `--at`, so `finished_at − started_at` is not `wall_s` (PR #52 contract review F5).
       - `stopped`: the deadline stopped the host's lane; `not_looked`: the host's `deferred` rows, every reason.
       - `held_until` and `doublings`: `Wire::slowed`, the latest hold the run's `Wire` read or raised while it was still ahead of the clock, and the host's doublings in this run (`a_wire_reports_each_hosts_doublings_and_the_hold_it_saw`; a hold read from an earlier run's pace file: `a_wire_reports_a_hold_an_earlier_run_left_beside_the_store`).
       - Run file (`newsvault.run/1`): `wall_s`, `busy[]` (`host`, `busy_s`) and `holds_capped[]` (`host`, `retry_after`, `held_until`; owner decision 2026-11-18, answer 1), and since PR 5 the collect's `source`; each optional (§10's run-file bullets).
       - Ledger day row: `wall_s`, `wall_share` of 86,400 s, `busy[]` per host with its `share`, `holds_capped`, `runs_lease_held`; the report's `busy_bound`, 0.5; `--check` findings `wall_time_over_bound` and `host_busy_over_bound` above 0.5, never at it; a `busy` human line per day and a `busy_host` line per host.
       - Tests: `every_busy_field_reaches_the_envelope_the_run_file_and_the_ledger`, every field a value no other field has (waited 21 s, requesting 9 s, busy 30 s, 3 requests, stopped, 1 not looked, held, 2 doublings, wall 36 s); `the_ledger_sums_busy_time_against_half_a_day_and_leaves_lease_held_runs_unobserved`.
       - Filtered by `--source` since 2026-11-18 (PR #52 answer 6, PR 5): a collect run file names its `source`, and under `--source` the day's `wall_s`, `busy[]` and `holds_capped` sum only that source's run files; a file naming none (before 2026-11-18, a robots refresh) counts only with no `--source` (`ledger.rs::the_ledger_filters_busy_time_by_the_run_files_source`: 41 s with none, 11 s and 13 s per source). `runs_*` counts stay unfiltered.
       - Unguarded: no live run has produced these numbers yet.
     - Deferred by the owner until the first live data: letting a lane whose slot is not due be skipped (§10's earliest-due-first row).
     - Unknown, VPS: the CPU architecture (the image is built for amd64 only), docker's version and whether this user may run it, systemd's version (`%G` needs 249 or later), free disk, NTP sync, linger, CPU headroom beside AlphaLens, node-exporter's textfile directory and Prometheus's rule files; for N1's copy (PR 7), whether the AIO container holds `sh`, `cat`, `wc`, `ls`, `mv`, `rm` and `mkdir`; the AIO Nextcloud version (`occ info:file` is in the source of stable27 to 32 and master, read 2026-11-18); whether AIO's daily backup or container update overlaps 22:55 to 23:26:30 UTC (AIO stops the containers for it, so the run would exit 7); whether AIO's own backup also takes the 7 copies; whether server-side encryption is on; no read-only check of these has run.
       - `occ files:scan --path` registers a `Backups` parent folder it has not seen: `Scanner::scanFile` scans a parent missing from the index first (`lib/private/Files/Cache/Scanner.php`, stable32, read 2026-11-18); not checked against the AIO version.
   - PR order (step-7 critique §D, 2026-11-17), each passing the gate alone:
     1. docs: these decisions.
     2. feat(collect): the `deferred` table, its migration and ledger count, with the cap of 150 as its first producer (`follow_up_cap`). Built 2026-11-18: §3.2's `deferred`, §11.2's cap bullet; tests and mutations below.
     3. feat(news-provider): condition A's core: a bounded reservation `PastDeadline { go_at }` that writes nothing, taken before `AttemptSlot::open`; the stopped host's lane drained into `deferred` (`run_deadline`, `host_held`); S7-D7's ceiling. Built 2026-11-18: the condition A bullets above, §3.2's `deferred`, §8 row R19 holes 11 and 12, §10's back-off rows. Its human-line test counts 3 `run_deadline`, 4 `host_held` and 0 `follow_up_cap`: with `follow_up_cap` alone the line's total and its per-reason count were always equal, so a swap went undetected (mutation review 2026-11-18).
     4. feat(newsvault): the schedule as data, S7-D3's deadline, S7-D11's busy fields, S7-D9's `lease_held` finish; and a minimum age of the RSS answer that sets the GNW walk's upper end, at least the shortest poll period in the schedule data (30 min), a construction (owner decision 2026-11-18, "Zgadzam sie"). Built 2026-11-18: §11.2's schedule bullets and GNW minimum age, the S7-D9 and S7-D11 rows above; mutations below.
     5. feat(collect): S7-D5's carried queue, the reader of `deferred`, with expiry. MUST land before the first live run (owner decision 2026-11-18): §11.6's 30-min replay walked up to 113 GNW ids in one poll, against 87 requests a 30-min window allows (870 s / 10 s). Built 2026-11-18 with the owner's answers of that day to PR #50 (question 1, `held`; c, rebuild through the provider) and PR #52 (1, the frontier by slot; 4, documents and walk ids carried, indexes not; 6, the run file's `source`): §3.2's `deferred_closed` and `held`, §11.2's GNW frontier bullets, the S7-D11 bullets above; mutations below.
     6. chore(deploy): the image, systemd user units under `deploy/`: oneshot, `OnCalendar … UTC`, `AccuracySec=1s`, `SuccessExitStatus=8`, the watchdog, a separate scheduler lock for `robots refresh`; installed by hand after the read-only VPS checks. The watchdog also alerts on a persistent `range_unread` (owner decision 2026-11-18). Built 2026-11-18 with the contact file and S7-D5's lane order: the PR 6 bullets below.
     7. chore(deploy): one pinned toolchain, the collect's exit metric, N1's daily Nextcloud copy (owner decisions 2026-11-18). Built 2026-11-18: the PR 7 bullets below.
     - PRs 2, 3 and 5 and the per-document robots check are the hard gates; the robots check is built (2026-11-17), PRs 2, 3 and 5 on 2026-11-18.
     - PR 6, built 2026-11-18 (files written and validated; nothing installed on the VPS, no tag pushed, no image published):
       - Image: `Dockerfile`, both stages `ubuntu:noble-20261024.1` pinned by digest, whose `libsqlite3-0` (3.45) is the SQLite the gate links (`bundled` is step 8): every workflow job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` and `ci.yml` checks its `libsqlite3-0` is 3.45; rustup-init 1.29.3 checked against its published sha256; the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml`'s, which `ci.yml` installs too (PR 7); `tini` as PID 1; user 10001; the hosts and rules files under `/usr/share/bellwether/`; no secret.
         - Rejected: an official `rust:*` builder: Debian bookworm's SQLite is 3.40, not CI's 3.45.
         - Rejected: `ci.yml` on `ubuntu-latest`: on 2026-11-18 that was 26.04 with SQLite 3.46.1 (run 44329718818's `Image: ubuntu-26.04`; Launchpad's resolute source 3.46.1-9ubuntu0.4), so the tests linked a library the image does not ship (PR #54 review).
       - Unpinned: the Ubuntu packages, installed at the version the archive serves on build day.
       - `.github/workflows/image.yml`: a pull request builds the image and runs it read-only as a non-root user (`schema`), checks `libsqlite3-0` is 3.45, and reads back `docker inspect`'s user and entrypoint (`10001`, tini); a `v*` tag does the same, then logs in to GHCR with the job's own token (`packages: write` on that job only) and pushes `ghcr.io/kamilpajak/bellwether-newsvault:<tag>`, printing the digest to the run summary. No third-party action: the runner's own docker. Built for amd64 only.
       - The publish job first requires the tag's commit to be an ancestor of `origin/main` and to have a successful `ci.yml` run on its SHA (`actions: read`): a workflow cannot `needs:` another, and a tag on any branch otherwise published an untested image.
       - `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`: `promtool check rules` and `promtool test rules` (`prom/prometheus:v3.15.1` by digest); `kubeconform -strict` v0.8.0, checksum-verified, against Kubernetes 1.34.12's schemas; `shellcheck` of the watchdog script and `systemd-analyze --user verify` of every unit.
       - The contact as a file (owner, 2026-11-17): each contact variable has a `_FILE` twin (`contact::read`); one trailing `\n` is removed. Refusals, none printing the value:

         | condition | code | exit |
         |---|---|---|
         | the variable and its `_FILE` twin both set | `invalid_argument` (`usage error:`) | 2 |
         | the named file absent | `input_absent` | 4 |
         | unreadable, a directory, or not UTF-8 | `io_failed` | 2 |
         | empty, or a lone `\n` | `credential_contact_missing` | 2 |
         | a control character after the one `\n` is removed (a second line, a `\r`) | `input_unparseable` | 2 |

       - S7-D5's lane order (owner decision 1, 2026-11-19, fresh-first): each lane of `HostQueue` holds two queues.
         - `fresh`: the planned index, the follow-ups it lists, the next sitemap page, walk ids and retries, in the order pushed; a retried carried request goes to the back of `fresh`.
         - `carried`: filled by `HostQueue::carry`, oldest `deferred_at` first; `pop` serves it only while the lane's `fresh` is empty.
         - Round-robin across lanes and counting at `pop` are unchanged; `HostQueue::stop` defers `fresh`, then `carried`.
         - Its gain is read as §8 row R22's `listing_latency`, fresh and carried apart; no production number yet (2026-11-19).
       - Units, `deploy/systemd/`, all USER units:
         - `newsvault-collect.timer`: one `OnCalendar=*-*-* HH:MM:00 UTC` per `SLOTS` entry, 31; `AccuracySec=1s`; no `RandomizedDelaySec`, no `Persistent=`, so a missed slot is not run late.
         - `newsvault-collect.service`: oneshot; `ExecStartPre` refuses a run unless `timedatectl` reports `NTPSynchronized=yes`, the kernel's flag whatever daemon sets it (the container reads the host's clock; rejected: a check inside the container, which has no `timedated`); `flock --nonblock --conflict-exit-code 7` on `%t/bellwether-store.lock`; `docker run --rm --name newsvault-collect --pull never --user %U:%G --read-only --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges`, the store at `~/.bellwether/vault` bind-mounted on `/vault` and the contact file bind-mounted `readonly`, both with `--mount type=bind`; `collect --source open_web_wires --window today --schedule open_web --expected-runs 31`; `SuccessExitStatus=8`; `TimeoutStartSec=2790`; no `Restart=`; `ExecStopPost` writes the run's result first (PR 7), then removes the container only when `SERVICE_RESULT` is `timeout`.
         - `--mount type=bind`, which refuses a run whose host path is missing. Rejected: `--volume`: it creates a missing host path as a root-owned directory (docs.docker.com, bind mounts), so a skipped install step left a `feed-contact` directory every run fails on (`io_failed`) and the user cannot remove.
         - `--pull never`: the image is pulled at install. Rejected: a pull at each run; a registry outage or a slow pull spends the slot's window.
         - `newsvault-robots-refresh.service`: started by hand only; the same lock and container flags; `TimeoutStartSec` held equal to the collect's.
         - The store lock is S7-D10's separate scheduler lock: the store's own lease already refuses a second writer (exit 7, `lease_held`); the outer lock decides before a container starts. Rejected: `flock --wait`; a waiting collect spends its own window.
         - `newsvault-watchdog.timer` (`*-*-* *:50:00 UTC`, hourly) and `.service`, running `deploy/systemd/newsvault-watchdog` installed as `~/.local/lib/bellwether/newsvault-watchdog`: `ledger --at <now> --from <yesterday> --to <today> --expected-runs 31 --check --format prometheus` in the image, the store bind-mounted `readonly`, `--network none`, written to `.newsvault.prom.<pid>` in `NEWSVAULT_TEXTFILE_DIR` and renamed to `newsvault.prom` for the ledger's exits 0, 1 and 8; any other exit leaves the last file, so the staleness alert fires. `--expected-runs 31`: a day with no run file states no expected count, so without it a day no run reached raised no `runs_below_expected`.
         - `newsvault.env`: the template of `~/.config/bellwether/newsvault.env`: `NEWSVAULT_IMAGE` (tag and digest) and `NEWSVAULT_TEXTFILE_DIR`; no secret.
           - Since 2026-11-19 `NEWSVAULT_TEXTFILE_DIR` is the placeholder `<node-exporter-textfile-directory>`, and the watchdog, backup and unit-result scripts refuse a value that is not an absolute path, exit 2 before docker runs or a file is written: `the_templates_textfile_placeholder_is_refused_by_every_script_before_it_runs` (mutations 2026-11-19, each script's check removed and the template a path again: each red). Rejected: a generic default path: the first install's directory was not the old default, and a path in a template reads as a fact about a machine.
       - Metrics: `newsvault ledger --format prometheus`, schema `newsvault.prom/1`, on stdout for the exits 0, 1 and 8 as JSON is; `cmd_ledger::PROMETHEUS_FAMILIES` lists the families. Rejected: a shell step converting the JSON with `jq`: the image holds no `jq`, and the conversion would be untested code beside the tested renderer.
       - Per-day families are labelled `source`, `run_date` and `days_ago` (0 today, 1 yesterday, from `--at`'s UTC date), so a rule can read yesterday's closed day apart from today's running one.
       - Each per-day rule is `max without(days_ago) (…)`, so one day's alert is labelled by `run_date` and keeps its labels across UTC midnight. Rejected: alerts labelled `days_ago`: at the 00:50 read one event resolved as `days_ago="0"` and fired again as `days_ago="1"`, a second notification its silence did not match (promtool v3.15.1, PR #54 review, 2026-11-18).
       - Alerts, `deploy/prometheus/newsvault.rules.yml`; severities `critical` and `warning` are placeholders for the VPS's Alertmanager routes:

         | alert | expr | severity | construction |
         |---|---|---|---|
         | `NewsvaultWatchdogStale` | `(time() - max(newsvault_ledger_at_timestamp_seconds{schema="newsvault.prom/1"})) > 7200`, or the series absent | critical | 2 × the watchdog's 3,600 s |
         | `NewsvaultCollectStale` | `newsvault_collect_last_asked_age_seconds > 10800` | critical | 2 × the longest poll period, 5,400 s: more than one slot missed |
         | `NewsvaultNeverAsked` | no request age while a read exists | critical | — |
         | `NewsvaultLedgerIncomplete` | `newsvault_ledger_complete == 0` | warning | ledger exit 8 |
         | `NewsvaultHostHeld` | `newsvault_deferred_host_held > 0` | critical | S7-D8 |
         | `NewsvaultDeferredExpired` | `newsvault_carried_expired > 0` | critical | S7-D8 |
         | `NewsvaultWalkRangeUnreadPersistent` | `newsvault_walk_range_unread_polls >= 2` | critical | a manual poll inside a slot reads one (§11.2's GNW frontier bullets); a changed link shape makes every scheduled poll one |
         | `NewsvaultHoldsCapped` | `newsvault_holds_capped > 0` | critical | S7-D7 |
         | `NewsvaultWallTimeOverBound`, `NewsvaultHostBusyOverBound` | the finding's count `> 0` | warning | S7-D11, owner 2026-11-18 |
         | `NewsvaultRunsMissed` | `runs_below_expected` with `days_ago="1"` | warning | today's count is short until its last slot |
         | `NewsvaultRowsZero` | `rows_committed_zero_against_vendor_rows` `> 0` | warning | §8 row R16 |
         | `NewsvaultCollectFailed` (PR 7) | the collect unit's last `SERVICE_RESULT` not `success`, or its exit status neither 0 nor 8 | critical | S7-D8; never on exit 8 |
         | `NewsvaultBackupStale` (PR 7) | `(time() - newsvault_backup_last_success_timestamp_seconds) > 174690`, or the series absent | critical | 2 × the backup's daily 86,400 s + its `TimeoutStartSec` 1,800 s + systemd's 90 s stop timeout: the stamp is the run's start and appears after its scan. Rejected: 172,800 alone: one missed day fired while the next run wrote its copy (`promtool`, 2026-11-18) |
         | `NewsvaultBackupFailed` (PR 7) | the backup unit's last `SERVICE_RESULT` not `success` | warning | exit 8: the copy is in and scanned, and old copies were not deleted or the second scan failed; any other exit: no copy that day, stale fires a day later |

         - An alert on a day's count fires while that day is in the two-day range, up to about 48 h after the event; a silence on its `run_date` acknowledges it.
         - No alert reads the findings `holds_a_failure` (any failed attempt, a 404 included) and `coverage_incomplete` (EDGAR, not deployed); both still reach `newsvault_check_findings`.
       - Kubernetes, `deploy/k8s/newsvault-collect.yaml`, not deployed: a PVC and two CronJobs, `0 2,5,8,11-22 * * *` and `30 0,3,6,9,11-21,23 * * *`, `timeZone: Etc/UTC`, whose union is `SLOTS` (one cron line cannot hold 31 slots); `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid`, `startingDeadlineSeconds: 60`, `activeDeadlineSeconds: 2790`, `backoffLimit: 0`, `restartPolicy: Never`; the contact from key `contact` of Secret `bellwether-feed-contact` (`items` names it), created by hand: `kubectl create secret generic bellwether-feed-contact --from-file=contact=<file>`.
         - Exit 8 as success: `podFailurePolicy` cannot do it; its actions are `FailJob`, `FailIndex`, `Ignore` and `Count`, and `Ignore` starts a replacement pod, a second run in the slot; `successPolicy` reads only succeeded indexes of an Indexed Job. So the pod's shell maps 8 to 0, the twin of `SuccessExitStatus=8`, under `tini -g` so a SIGTERM reaches newsvault; `podFailurePolicy` names exits 2, 4 and 7 `FailJob`, so the Job's condition names the code.
         - Two CronJobs share no `concurrencyPolicy`: on one node, a run past its next slot meets the store lease and exits 7, as two systemd starts cannot (a timer elapsing on an active unit starts nothing). On several nodes the `ReadWriteOnce` claim cannot attach to a second node, so that pod waits in `ContainerCreating` until `activeDeadlineSeconds`.
       - Tests: `services/newsvault/tests/deploy.rs` (`the_collect_timer_fires_at_exactly_the_schedules_slots_in_utc`, `every_run_bound_is_the_longest_window_plus_two_minutes`, `the_collect_unit_succeeds_on_exit_8_never_restarts_and_mounts_the_contact`, `the_kubernetes_cron_lines_cover_exactly_the_slots_and_share_one_job`, `every_metric_the_watchdog_writes_has_an_alert_rule_and_no_rule_reads_another`, `each_staleness_threshold_is_two_of_the_intervals_it_watches`, `the_collect_and_refresh_units_share_one_lock_and_one_container_shape`, `the_watchdog_checks_yesterday_and_today_with_every_slot_expected`, `no_unit_or_script_mounts_a_host_path_with_volume`, `every_workflow_job_runs_on_the_images_ubuntu_release`, and since the PR #54 mutation review: `the_deploy_workflow_runs_each_validator_on_its_file`, `only_a_tag_on_a_green_main_commit_publishes_and_only_that_job_writes_packages`, `the_image_runs_as_user_10001_under_tini`, `every_service_is_a_oneshot_reading_the_env_file`, `a_hung_watchdog_is_stopped_before_its_next_firing`, `each_timer_starts_its_own_service`, `the_collect_container_is_removed_read_only_unprivileged_and_never_pulled`, `a_timed_out_container_is_removed_by_its_own_name_and_a_collect_needs_a_synced_clock`, `the_collect_unit_asks_what_each_kubernetes_job_asks`, `each_kubernetes_job_maps_exit_8_to_0_and_holds_its_limits`, `the_watchdog_renames_a_hidden_file_for_0_1_and_8_and_keeps_the_last_file_otherwise`, `the_watchdog_reads_at_the_utc_now_whatever_the_hosts_zone`);          1. Read-only checks: `uname -m` (`x86_64`: the image is amd64 only); `docker version` and `id -nG` (the user may run docker); `systemctl --version` (249 or later for `%G`); `loginctl show-user "$USER" --property=Linger`; `timedatectl show --property=NTPSynchronized --value`; `df -h ~`; `nproc` and `uptime`; node-exporter's `--collector.textfile.directory` and whether this user can write there; where Prometheus reads rule files.
         2. Owner: tag `vX.Y.Z` on main and push the tag (`v0.1.0` first, proposed 2026-11-18); `image.yml` pushes the image and prints `ghcr.io/kamilpajak/bellwether-newsvault@sha256:…`. After this first publish, set the package public: GitHub, the package `bellwether-newsvault`, Package settings, Change visibility, Public (owner decision 2026-11-18). The VPS then pulls with no `docker login`.
         3. Install: `install -d -m 0700 ~/.config/bellwether ~/.bellwether/vault ~/.local/lib/bellwether`; `install -d ~/.config/systemd/user`; the contact typed into `install -m 0600 /dev/stdin ~/.config/bellwether/feed-contact` (never an argument: the shell history keeps it); `install -m 0600 deploy/systemd/newsvault.env ~/.config/bellwether/` and set `NEWSVAULT_IMAGE` and `NEWSVAULT_TEXTFILE_DIR` (step 4 checks the `NEXTCLOUD_*` four); `install -m 0755 deploy/systemd/newsvault-watchdog deploy/systemd/newsvault-backup deploy/systemd/newsvault-unit-result ~/.local/lib/bellwether/`; `install -m 0644 deploy/systemd/*.service deploy/systemd/*.timer ~/.config/systemd/user/`; `docker pull "<image>@<digest>"`; root: `loginctl enable-linger <user>`; `systemctl --user daemon-reload`; `deploy/prometheus/newsvault.rules.yml` into Prometheus's rule files and a reload.
         4. The Nextcloud copy (N1, PR 7); no app password:
            - Read-only checks of the four `NEXTCLOUD_*` values in `~/.config/bellwether/newsvault.env`: `docker ps --filter name=nextcloud-aio-nextcloud --format '{{.Names}}'` prints the container; `docker inspect --format '{{range .Mounts}}{{.Destination}} {{end}}' nextcloud-aio-nextcloud` lists `/mnt/ncdata`; `docker exec -u www-data nextcloud-aio-nextcloud php occ user:list` lists the user id that replaces the template's `NEXTCLOUD_USER` placeholder; any other value that differs from the template goes into the env file too.
            - Read-only checks of this copy's half of the "Unknown, VPS" bullet above: `docker exec -u www-data nextcloud-aio-nextcloud sh -c 'command -v cat wc ls mv rm mkdir'` prints six paths; `docker exec -u www-data nextcloud-aio-nextcloud php occ status` prints the version; `php occ list info` lists `info:file`; `php occ encryption:status` reads `enabled: false`; AIO's interface shows the daily backup and update time, outside 22:55 to 23:26:30 UTC.
            - The textfile directory of step 1 then also receives `newsvault-backup.prom` (the backup), `newsvault-collect-result.prom` and `newsvault-backup-result.prom` (the units' `ExecStopPost`).
            - Once after step 6's collect: `systemctl --user start newsvault-backup.service`, then `journalctl --user -u newsvault-backup.service -o cat`; go on at exit 0; then `Backups/bellwether` shows one `bellwether-vault-*.tar.gz` in Nextcloud's web Files and, once the Mac client syncs that folder, on the Mac.
         5. Manual robots refresh: `systemctl --user start newsvault-robots-refresh.service`, then `journalctl --user -u newsvault-robots-refresh.service -o cat`; go on only at exit 0 (1: an admitted pin differs, so the hosts file changes first).
         6. One manual collect: `systemctl --user start newsvault-collect.service`; exit 0 or 8 (`systemctl --user status`); the run file under `~/.bellwether/vault/runs/`; `newsvault-collect-result.prom` in the textfile directory.
         7. Enable: `systemctl --user enable --now newsvault-collect.timer newsvault-watchdog.timer newsvault-backup.timer`; `systemctl --user list-timers` shows the next slot and 22:55 in UTC.
         8. After the first `:50`: `newsvault.prom` in the textfile directory and `newsvault_ledger_at_timestamp_seconds` in Prometheus.
       - First run results, 2026-11-19 (`v0.1.0`):

         | step | result |
         |---|---|
         | 5, robots refresh, 09:36:31Z | all five pinned robots matched; CNW's robots.txt 200 |

     - PR 7, built 2026-11-18 (owner decisions of that day, "zgadzam sie z Twoimi propozycjami"; the backup's way amended the same day to a direct write, "tak, sprawdz", N1; files written and validated, nothing installed, no tag pushed):
       - One toolchain: `rust-toolchain.toml` (`1.99.1`, `clippy` and `rustfmt`) is its only home. `ci.yml` runs `rustup toolchain install` and checks `rustc --version` against the file; the Dockerfile installs rustup with `--default-toolchain none`, copies the file and runs the same command. Rejected: `ci.yml` floating with the runner image: the image compiled with 1.99.1 and the gate with whatever the runner shipped.
       - The collect's exit: `deploy/systemd/newsvault-unit-result <label>`, the first `ExecStopPost` of the collect and backup units, prefixed `-`; it writes `newsvault_unit_result_success` (1 when `SERVICE_RESULT` is `success`), `newsvault_unit_exit_status` (`EXIT_STATUS` when `EXIT_CODE` is `exited`, else -1) and `newsvault_unit_result_timestamp_seconds`, labelled `unit`, to a dot-file renamed onto `newsvault-<label>-result.prom`.
         - First, so a failing container removal cannot keep it from running; `-`, so a metric write never changes the unit's result.
       - `newsvault snapshot --out <file>` (`cmd_snapshot`): takes the store's lease read-only (`store::hold_lease_to_copy`; `flock` needs no write access, so the store stays a read-only mount), opens the commit log read-only, copies every page in one `sqlite3_backup_step(-1)`, runs `PRAGMA quick_check` on the copy and renames it from `.<name>.partial`. Envelope `newsvault.snapshot/1`: `store`, `out`, `user_version`, `bytes`, `dry_run`, `changed`. `--dry-run` checks the store, the lease and `--out`.
         - One step, because a step of N pages restarts whenever another connection commits (SQLite's backup docs), so a busy store may never finish.
         - Rejected: `VACUUM INTO`, which rebuilds every b-tree instead of copying pages; the host's `sqlite3` 3.45.1 `.backup`: N1's table.
       - Refusals, each an object on stderr with stdout empty:

         | condition | code | exit |
         |---|---|---|
         | no `commit.sqlite3` in the store; `--out`'s directory absent | `input_absent` | 4 |
         | `--out` exists | `invalid_argument` | 2 |
         | a writer holds the lease; a writer outside it keeps the log locked past 30 s | `lease_held` (retryable) | 7 |
         | the copy is `SQLITE_CORRUPT` or `quick_check` answers other than `ok` | `input_unparseable` | 2 |
         | any other SQLite or filesystem failure, among them a hot `commit.sqlite3-journal` a killed collect left, which a read-only mount cannot roll back ("attempt to write a readonly database", measured 2026-11-18, Mac, directory mode 555; the next collect rolls it back), and a WAL store (§6) | `io_failed` | 2 |

       - The backup: `newsvault-backup.timer` at `22:55:00 UTC`, `AccuracySec=1s`, no `Persistent=` and no `RandomizedDelaySec`; `newsvault-backup.service`, oneshot, `flock --nonblock --conflict-exit-code 7` on the collect's `%t/bellwether-store.lock`, `TimeoutStartSec=1800`.
         - The time, a construction: each slot holds the lock at most its start + `TimeoutStartSec` (2,790 s) + systemd's default stop timeout (90 s); the longest gap that leaves before the next slot is 2,520 s, after each 90-min slot and after 22:00. Of those gaps, the one after 22:00 (22:48 to 23:30) takes the copy right after the US session; 22:55 + 1,800 s + 90 s = 23:26:30.
         - `--nonblock`: a backup that meets a run (a manual refresh, a hung collect) exits 7 and `NewsvaultBackupFailed` fires. Rejected: waiting: a backup that started late would still hold the lock at 23:30 and cost a collect slot.
         - No `Persistent=`: a backup run late at boot could take the lock over a slot; two missed days are `NewsvaultBackupStale`, one is not.
       - The script, `deploy/systemd/newsvault-backup`: refuses an unsafe `NEXTCLOUD_*` value by name before any docker call (2, below); runs `newsvault snapshot` in the image (`--read-only`, `--network none`, `--cap-drop ALL`, the store mounted `readonly`, only a `mktemp -d` work directory under `~/.cache/bellwether` writable); archives the snapshot as `commit.sqlite3` with every other top-level store entry, hidden ones included, but `commit.sqlite3`, `commit.sqlite3-*` and `LOCK`, as `bellwether-vault-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>.tar.gz`.
         - Every container call is `docker exec -u www-data <container>`, the write alone with `-i`; no `--privileged`, no root, no `sudo`. With `<dir>` = `$NEXTCLOUD_DATA/$NEXTCLOUD_USER/files/$NEXTCLOUD_FOLDER`, in order:

           | step | call | on failure |
           |---|---|---|
           | folder | `mkdir -p -- <dir>` | exit 7 |
           | write | `sh -c 'cat >"$1" && wc -c <"$1"' sh <dir>/.<name>.part`, the archive on stdin | exit 7 |
           | size | the printed byte count against the archive's size | exit 7, not renamed |
           | rename | `mv -- <dir>/.<name>.part <dir>/<name>`, one `rename(2)` in one directory | exit 7 |
           | scan | `php occ files:scan --path=$NEXTCLOUD_USER/files/$NEXTCLOUD_FOLDER` | exit 7 only when `occ` itself fails: `files:scan` exits 0 on an unknown user, a path it cannot find and every other error it catches (`apps/files/lib/Command/Scan.php`, stable30 to 32 and master, read 2026-11-18) |
           | lookup | `php occ info:file /$NEXTCLOUD_USER/files/$NEXTCLOUD_FOLDER/<name>`, which exits 1 on a file Nextcloud does not know (`core/Command/Info/File.php`, same reading) | exit 7 |
           | metric | below | exit 2 |
           | list | `ls -1A -- <dir>` | exit 8, nothing deleted |
           | delete | one `rm -f --` of the oldest names of the copy's shape beyond 7 and of every `.<copy's shape>.part` a killed run left | exit 8 |
           | scan again | only after a delete, so Nextcloud drops the deleted files; an error it catches still exits 0, and the next run's first scan of the folder drops them (`Scanner::handleChildren`, not run) | exit 8 |

         - The `EXIT` trap removes the work directory and, until the rename, the staging file in the container; `trap 'exit 130' INT TERM` makes dash run it on systemd's SIGTERM (without it dash dies and leaves both, measured 2026-11-18 by the PR #56 review, `/bin/dash` on the Mac).
         - Nothing is deleted unless the copy just written is the newest name of the copy's shape in the listing (exit 8, naming the newest). Rejected: "the listing holds the copy": a copy whose name sorts after it (a clock that ran ahead, a file put there by hand) made the new copy the oldest; the PR #56 review's run against the stub with seven `20990101`-to-`20990107` copies exited 0 with only those seven left. Names match the shape whole (`grep -x`).
         - Rejected: renaming into place before the size check: a short write would sit under the final name. Rejected: the staging suffix `.partial`: Nextcloud's scanner indexes it, so a killed run's full-size file entered the index at the next run's first scan; `Scanner::isPartialFile` skips `.part` (stable32, read 2026-11-18).
         - Metric: `newsvault_backup_last_success_timestamp_seconds` (the snapshot's start) and `newsvault_backup_last_success_bytes`, written after the lookup to a dot-file renamed onto `newsvault-backup.prom`; a failed write, rename, scan or lookup leaves the last one.
       - `newsvault.env` gains `NEXTCLOUD_CONTAINER` (`nextcloud-aio-nextcloud`), `NEXTCLOUD_DATA` (`/mnt/ncdata`), `NEXTCLOUD_USER` (a placeholder the script refuses until it is set on the VPS: hostnames and login ids stay out of this public repository) and `NEXTCLOUD_FOLDER` (`Backups/bellwether`), the 2026-11-18 values; no secret.
         - The script refuses each by name, exit 2, before any docker call: unset or empty; `NEXTCLOUD_DATA` not absolute; `NEXTCLOUD_FOLDER` absolute; `/` in the container or user; a trailing `/` or an empty part; a part not starting with a letter or digit (so `..` and hidden names); any character outside letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`.
       - `deploy.yml` runs `shellcheck` over the three scripts and installs the two new ones before `systemd-analyze verify`.
       - Tests: `snapshot.rs` (`a_copy_taken_while_another_connection_commits_is_one_whole_commit_at_the_stores_version`: a writer commits 50 rows at a time throughout the copy, and the copy passes `integrity_check`, holds a multiple of 50 and the store's `user_version`; `a_copy_started_while_a_transaction_has_spilled_into_the_file_holds_that_whole_commit`: a writer's open transaction has already written pages into the file, and the copy waits on its lock and holds the whole commit; `a_dry_run_reports_the_stores_version_and_writes_nothing`; `each_refusal_exits_by_its_code_with_stdout_empty`; `a_damaged_store_is_refused_as_malformed_and_nothing_is_left`; `a_freelist_count_the_pages_disagree_with_is_named_by_the_copys_quick_check`; `every_option_the_snapshot_parser_takes_is_in_the_machine_readable_schema`); `deploy.rs` (`the_backup_runs_once_a_day_inside_the_longest_gap_the_collect_runs_leave`, `the_backup_staleness_threshold_is_two_daily_intervals_plus_one_runs_bound`, `the_backup_unit_takes_the_store_lock_without_waiting_and_records_its_result`, `one_rust_toolchain_is_named_only_in_rust_toolchain_toml`, the backup script under `/bin/dash` against a stub `docker` that maps `/mnt/ncdata` onto a temporary directory and runs each container command on the host, and a stub `mv`: `the_backup_writes_through_the_container_renames_scans_then_keeps_the_seven_newest`, `the_folder_keeps_exactly_the_seven_newest_copies_whatever_it_held`, `every_container_call_runs_as_www_data_and_only_the_write_reads_stdin`, `a_short_write_is_never_renamed_and_its_staging_file_is_removed`, `a_failed_mkdir_write_rename_or_scan_exits_7_by_name_and_deletes_nothing`, `a_failed_retention_exits_8_after_the_metric_and_a_bad_listing_deletes_nothing`, `a_copy_sorting_after_the_new_one_stops_the_retention_and_deletes_nothing`, `a_terminated_run_exits_130_and_removes_its_work_directory_and_staging_file`, `an_unsafe_nextcloud_setting_is_refused_by_name_before_any_docker_call`, `the_backup_writes_its_metric_through_a_hidden_file_renamed_after_the_scan`, `a_refused_snapshot_stops_before_any_container_call`, `every_run_removes_its_staging_copy_whether_it_succeeds_or_fails`, `the_backup_container_reads_the_store_read_only_with_no_network`; `the_unit_result_names_success_and_the_exit_status_through_a_renamed_hidden_file`; and, renamed from the watchdog-only test, `every_metric_written_has_an_alert_rule_and_no_rule_reads_another`); `newsvault.rules.test.yml` (exit 0 and 8 no alert, exit 2, -1, a success exiting 7 and a failed result exiting 0 alert; the backup fresh at 48 h, stale at 49 h and when absent; one missed day quiet while the next run writes its copy; two missed days quiet at 174,600 s and stale at 174,900 s).
       - A rename onto the target is read through a hard link to the old file, whose text must stay the old one; a write into the target changes it.

### 11.6 Evidence, 2026-11-14 (Saturday), 2026-11-16 (Monday), 2026-11-17 (Tuesday) and 2026-11-19 (Thursday)

- Method: `curl -sS --compressed -A 'research-feed-probe/0.1' -o <body> -D <hdr> --max-time 30 <url>`; body = whitespace-collapsed `<p>` text in the host's container, or JSON-LD `articleBody`, parsed offline with Python's `html.parser`; 13 article fetches: 11 reached, 2 blocked.
- Spacing ≥ 5 s between requests to one host, ≤ 3 per host per probe run: below §11.1's 10 s floor, and Crawl-delay was not read before every fetch.

| host | request start (UTC) | status | body chars | rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| prnewswire.com (2 fetches) | 19:36:58; 19:37:18 | 200 | 9,463; 19,337 | `<p>` in `<article>` |
| rss.globenewswire.com | 19:36:54 | 200 | 5,117 | `<p>` in `div.main-body-container` |
| cnbc.com (2 fetches) | 19:37:19; 19:37:49 | 200 | 6,891; 4,910 | `<p>` in `div.ArticleBody-articleBody` |
| fool.com (article; transcript) | 19:37:51; 19:37:59 | 200 | 4,467; 28,038 | `<p>` in `<main>` |
| businessinsider.com (2 fetches) | 19:37:51; 19:37:59 | 200 | 2,281; 3,719 | JSON-LD `articleBody` |
| finance.yahoo.com (GNW release; Fool copy) | 19:37:52; 19:38:00 | 200 | 5,395; 5,174 | `<p>` in `<article>` |
| benzinga.com; marketbeat.com | 19:36:59; 19:37:49 | 403 | 0 | Cloudflare block page; Cloudflare JS challenge |

- CNBC sitemap vs page, 2 of 2 checked: Berkshire story sitemap `09:02:39-04:00` (13:02:39Z), page `article:published_time` 14:02:39Z, a wrong offset (EDT in EST season); FAA story sitemap `13:32:47-04:00` (17:32:47Z), page `datePublished` 09:26:23Z, `dateModified` 18:32:47Z, so the sitemap holds the modification time, with the wrong offset.
- Yahoo sitemap, 19:20:57Z: 1,000 rows over 18.27 h; provider keys from `image:loc` (`media.zenfs.com/en/<key>/`): `cnwgroup.com` 37, `prnewswire.com` 36, `globenewswire.com` 26, `business-wire.com` 6, `accesswire.ca` 6.
- GNW RSS, 19:22:19Z: 20 items over 3.93 h.
- PRN RSS, `date` 19:21:04 GMT: 20 items over 5.02 h (14:00-19:01).
- PRN's committed rule is `<p>` in `section.release-body`, not the row's `<article>` (§11.1). The same two probe bodies, each method run 2026-11-16:

| method | 302939553 paragraphs | 302939553 chars | 302938569 paragraphs | 302938569 chars |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the probe method above, `<p>` in `<article>` (the row's rule, reproduced) | not recorded | 9,463 | not recorded | 19,337 |
| the probe method above, `<p>` in `section.release-body` | 40 | 9,421 | 62 | 19,295 |
| `OpenWebFeed::extract` under the committed rules file, the `\n` between paragraphs counted, with the `br` space (§11.1) | 39 | 9,409 | 62 | 19,307 |

  - 302939553's 40th probe paragraph is `_______________________`, which holds no letter or digit, so the extractor drops it (§11.1). Before the `br` space, the extractor gave 9,397 and 19,295 chars.

**Weekday probe (probe6), 2026-11-16 (Monday), 08:04–23:30Z.**

- Construction: a one-off Python stdlib script, not kept (CONTRIBUTING rule 3); pace `max(Crawl-delay, 10 s)` per host; robots.txt read through `newsvault::robots` before any other request to a host; one GET per index per §11.2 slot, the period table's schedule; two extra Yahoo sitemap GETs at 19:20Z and 22:36Z, the clock times of the 2026-11-14 pair; all times UTC.
- Run record: 26 of 26 poll slots 08:00–23:30Z ran (08:00 started 268.851 s late); the 5 slots 00:30–06:30Z were missed because the process was not running; no sleep gap above 5 s.
- Index window (§11.2's period table): item key = https + lowercased host + path; publication value = RSS `pubDate` or `news:publication_date`; window = newest − oldest; the first poll per index is the baseline and yields no first sighting.
- Distinct items dated 2026-11-16 seen over the day: GNW 466, PRN 479, CNW 157 (sitemap and RSS alike), Fed 0, Yahoo 5,591. For PRN and GNW this is a lower bound: first sightings per hour, by the hour of the poll that first listed the item (2 polls an hour, 20 items each, so 40 is the most a hour can show):

  | hour | 11 | 12 | 13 | 14 | 15 | 16 | 17 | 18 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | GNW | 40 | 35 | 40 | 40 | 40 | 40 | 40 | 36 |
  | PRN | 33 | 36 | 40 | 40 | 40 | 39 | 40 | 26 |

- Business Wire `https://feeds.businesswire.com/BW/Earnings_News-rss`, one GET per poll slot after its robots.txt answered 200: 26 of 26 answered 200; 25 of 26 well-formed RSS (complete body, parses, root `rss`, a channel, at least 1 item); the one other, the 08:04Z poll, was a 1,048 B RSS with no item; longest run of failures 1; body 1,048 / 17,849 / 46,169 B (min / median / max); items 1 at 09:30Z, 38 at 23:30Z. No stability threshold was fixed beforehand, so only counts are reported.
- Article bodies: the 5 newest items of each host's first index fetch, one GET each. Plausible container, fixed before the data: one element holding ≥ 70% of the page's paragraph text (`<p>` text with a letter or a digit, outside `script`/`style`/`noscript`/`template`), ≥ 3 paragraphs and ≥ 500 chars. No selector is decided here.

  | host | plausible | deepest element holding ≥ 70% of the page's paragraph text | its share of the page's paragraph text |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | CNW (sitemap items) | 5 of 5 | `div.release-content` | 0.8474–0.9452 |
  | Fed | 0 of 5 | none passes; `div#article` holds 0.2128–0.5633; a navigation list `ul.nav.navbar-nav` holds 40 paragraphs, 978 chars, on every page | — |
  | Business Insider | 5 of 5 | `section.post-body-content.post-story-body-content`; JSON-LD `articleBody` present on 5 of 5 | 0.9975–0.9986 |

- Busy time, a projection and not a measurement: (index requests + new items of GNW, PRN, CNW sitemap and Fed + new Yahoo rows under the `business-wire.com` or `accesswire.ca` key) × 10 s, serially, against the 2026-11-16 rule (stop deadline = half the period, serial order) and the cap of 150 (§11.2); under round-robin and S7-D3 it is replaced by §11.2's completeness cost bullets. Over the 900 s deadline in 3 of 26 polls, below; the most follow-ups in any poll is 105, below the cap:

  | poll | follow-ups | projected busy s |
  |---|---|---|
  | 13:30 | 105 | 1,100 |
  | 14:30 | 99 | 1,040 |
  | 15:00 | 103 | 1,080 |

  - A lower bound: GNW and PRN add at most 20 follow-ups each per poll, their index size, and both reached 20 in all 3 rows above; no retry is counted, where §11.2's worst case multiplies by 1 + `MAX_TRANSIENT_RETRIES`.

**Yahoo, 2026-11-16 (Monday).**

- The 3-poll rows below were taken by 11:24Z, before 08:00 ET (13:00Z) and before the NYSE session (14:30–21:00Z): news-sitemap polls at 08:04:40, 09:30:00 and 11:00:02Z, article fetches at 10:52–11:24Z. Rows marked weekday probe span 08:04–23:30Z (above).
- The 2026-11-14 rows are a Saturday.
- Lag during weekday US hours (13:00–21:00Z, the research's window) is unmeasured.
- Sitemap window: each poll holds 1,000 rows; the span is newest minus oldest `news:publication_date`. The weekday probe's 26 polls: §11.2's period table.
- The 2026-11-14 22:36Z row is a Saturday probe body, recomputed 2026-11-16; the 19:20Z one (18.27 h) is above.

  | poll (UTC) | oldest – newest `publication_date` | span |
  |---|---|---|
  | 2026-11-14 22:36Z | not recorded | 20.23 h |
  | 2026-11-16 08:04:40 | 11-15 11:38 – 11-16 08:00 | 20.37 h |
  | 2026-11-16 09:30:00 | 11-15 15:12 – 11-16 09:24 | 18.21 h |
  | 2026-11-16 11:00:02 | 11-15 20:46 – 11-16 10:58 | 14.20 h |

- Turnover between consecutive polls, rows keyed by URL:

  | pair | elapsed | rows in common | rows replaced | replaced per hour |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 2026-11-14 19:20Z → 22:36Z | 3.26 h | 882 | 118 | about 36 |
  | 2026-11-16 08:04Z → 09:30Z | 1.42 h | 824 | 176 | 123.7 |
  | 2026-11-16 09:30Z → 11:00Z | 1.50 h | 720 | 280 | 186.6 |
  | 2026-11-16 14:00Z → 14:30Z, weekday probe, the day's highest rate | 0.50 h | 576 | 424 | 848.44 |
  | 2026-11-16 19:20Z → 22:36Z, weekday probe, the Saturday row's clock times | 3.27 h | 153 | 847 | 259.29 |

  - Of the rows replaced, the oldest left first: 840 of 847 on Monday 19:20→22:36Z, 117 of 118 on Saturday (removed rows among the earlier fetch's oldest by `publication_date`). 58 of Monday's 847 added rows carry a `publication_date` before the earlier fetch's newest row (Saturday: 19 of 118). The same code reproduced the Saturday counts from the saved 2026-11-14 bodies.

  - 3 rows of the second 2026-11-16 pair left although newer than the next poll's oldest row: one URL retitled, one moved to another path under the same numeric suffix, one removed. So a Yahoo URL is not a stable identity.
  - 32 and 38 URLs first appeared in the later poll of each 2026-11-16 pair although their `publication_date` predates the earlier poll; the delay after that date reached 48.7 and 689.8 min (lower bounds).
- Coverage of the direct wires, union of the 3 polls (1,456 URLs); a wire item counts when its `pubDate` lies between 2026-11-15T11:38Z (the first poll's oldest row) and 09:00Z (the last poll minus 2 h); matched by equal normalised title (HTML unescaped, lowercase, non-alphanumeric runs collapsed); English by `dc:language` for GNW and, for PRN and CNW, which carry no language field, by a title whose every code point is below U+2000 and that holds an English stopword (`cov2.py`):

  | wire | eligible | found on Yahoo | English | English found on Yahoo | non-English found on Yahoo |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | PRN | 36 | 17 | 18 | 12 | 5 of 18, each an English title the heuristic rejects (`€`, or no stopword) |
  | GNW | 35 | 23 | 24 | 23 | 0 of 11 |
  | CNW | 30 | 30 | 26 | 26 | 4 of 4, each an English title the heuristic rejects |

  - Found on Yahoo: `coverage` in `out.json`; non-English found = found minus English found; titles read 2026-11-16 by re-running `cov.py`'s parse over the same probe6 bodies.
  - So no non-English PRN or CNW title was found on Yahoo: every rejected title found is English, and PRN's Spanish and German titles the heuristic calls English are misses.
  - 2 of PRN's 6 English misses are the heuristic's errors (a Spanish and a German title), so PRN is 12 of 16.
  - PRN and GNW RSS hold 20 items each, so their eligible items run from 07:30Z on 2026-11-16 (oldest 07:31Z and 07:30Z), about 1.5 h; CNW's sitemap reaches back to 2026-11-13.
  - Fed: 0 of 20 Fed items in each of the 3 polls, matched by the first 50 normalised title characters against all 3 sitemaps.
  - 12 GNW law-firm notices of 10:46–10:47Z were absent from the 11:00Z poll, and no Yahoo title of the union starts with `investor notice` or holds `securities fraud` or `class action`; exclusion and lag cannot be told apart from one batch.
- Latency: Yahoo's `publication_date` copies the wire's minute (median difference 0 min over 17 PRN, 23 GNW and 30 CNW matches), so it is not an observation time. By first listing poll, 6 items (3 PRN, 3 GNW, all wire time 08:00Z) reached Yahoo one poll after the wire's own index: more than about 4 and at most 86 min later.

**PRN and GNW indexes at a 5-min period (probe10), 2026-11-17 (Tuesday), 12:00–16:00Z.**

- Construction: a one-off Python stdlib script, not kept (CONTRIBUTING rule 3); approved by the owner 2026-11-17 (`PG-D7`) for at most 288 requests; 48 cycles, one every 5 min from 12:00Z; pace `max(Crawl-delay, 10 s)` per host; robots read from probe6's saved bodies before each request; no redirect followed; `www.globenewswire.com` never asked; counts by the probe's own analysis script over its fetch log, 2026-11-17, unless a row says otherwise.
  - Each cycle: PRN news sitemap page 1, and page 2 only when page 1 shared no key with the previous cycle's union; PRN RSS; GNW RSS; GNW ids: the 2 lowest ids above an earlier frontier (the highest id an RSS poll showed) that no RSS poll showed, each asked at least 10 min after the frontier passed it, at `https://rss.globenewswire.com/news-release/<y>/<m>/<d>/<id>/0/en/x.html`.
- Run record: 158 requests, all 200, 0 lost, 0 robots skips; 48 of 48 cycles ran, starting at most 0.017 s late. PRN 97 (49 sitemap, 48 RSS), GNW 61 (48 RSS, 13 ids).

| page-1 generation stamp moving backwards between consecutive fetches | 0 times |

- Limits:

  - 13 of 13 ids at 200 bounds the share of ids not public 10 min after the frontier passed them at 20.6%, the exact one-sided 95% bound 1 − 0.05^(1/13) (3/13, 23%, by the rule of three); the sample is not random: the 2 lowest unshown ids of the newest band at least 10 min old, older bands skipped (the probe's id planner);
